v8 (hero video): screen-1 background replaced with hero-honey-bg.* (webm/mp4, -lite for <=1000px, poster jpg). Source: first 15 s of IMG_8581 2.mov, 4x motion-interpolated slow motion, 4 s crossfade from end back to start baked in. Loop length 55.9 s. Playback rate set to 1 in script. ritual-bg.* files removed.

## HoniOra V3 Lite Desktop

This is a new, separately-versioned branch, forked from the original-brand HONIORA_site_vNNN line at v399 (plus the v400 fix below). It carries the same original-brand content as that line (full Jenerise Cr.01 creatine ingredient story, Steve and Rachael Jennings, SGS purity verification) but changes how the site's navigation behaves: the compact "lite" nav -- burger menu, mini gold reserve hexes flanking the centred wordmark, and the sliding two-column link-list panel -- is now the site's permanent default at every screen width, including ordinary desktop monitors (1440px, 1920px, etc.), not just narrow/tablet viewports. The original full-width text-link hex nav (every section linked directly on the honeycomb header background) is still in the file's CSS, just permanently hidden rather than deleted, in case a future version wants the choice back.

This branch is versioned independently of HoniOra V1, V2, and the vNNN patch line -- a patch to one does not automatically apply to the others. See the Version section below for what's changed within this branch itself.

## What's in this package

- `index.html` — the entire site (markup, CSS, and JS all in one file).
- Every image, video, and icon file — hero shots, the hero glass video (`hero-glass.mp4`/`.webm` for desktop, `hero-glass-lite.mp4`/`.webm` for the lite site), the "In The Mix" Stack section banner (`matrix-stack-header.jpg`), nav hex buttons, pillar icons, the nav bar wordmark (`honiora-logo.png`), the Mānuka vista banner, the Rita Rocks feature photos (`rita-ora-feature.jpg` for desktop, `rita-ora-feature-lite.jpg` for the lite site), the Single Pack product shot in the store section (`plan-tube-single.jpg`), the store section's 5-tube gift box hero shot (`hero-packshot-cutout.png` / `hero-packshot-cutout-lite.png`), the HoniBlog Article 01 founder photo (`blog-founder-happy-place.jpg`), Article 02 video still (`blog-olive-oil-ozempic.jpg`), Article 03 video still (`blog-rhodiola.jpg`), Article 04 video still (`blog-pomegranate-benefits.jpg`), Article 05 video still (`blog-kiwi-slimland.jpg`), Article 06 video still (`blog-jenerise-womens-health.jpg`), Article 07 video still (`blog-manuka-benefits.jpg`), the Jenerise Cr.01 logo in the Creatine Dose section header (`jenerise-cr01-logo.png`), the Steve and Rachael Jennings portrait circles in the Creatine Dose section (`steve_jennings_portrait.jpg` / `-lite.jpg`, `rachael_jennings_portrait.jpg` / `-lite.jpg`), the spinning wax-seal medallion in the dead centre of the hero (`bee-seal-badge.png` / `-lite.png`, a transparent-background PNG), the full-bleed halved-pomegranate backdrop behind the Pomma+ & Urolithin A section (`pomma_pomegranate_bg.jpg` for desktop, `pomma_pomegranate_bg-lite.jpg` for the lite site), all 14 ingredient photos used in the Stack section (including `bifidobacterium_adolescentis.jpg`), and the cut-out lemon slice graphic used in the Lemon Water Upgrade section (`lemon-slice-cutout.png`, a transparent-background PNG), the single-tablet product shot used twice in the rolling-tablet animation at the top of 02 / The Protocol (`honifizz-tablet-single.jpg`, added at v98; the original two-tablet shot `honifizz-tablets.jpg` is still in the folder but no longer referenced) — sits flat in the same folder as `index.html`, referenced as plain filenames like `hero-glass.jpg` or `cGP-Pro_blackcurrant.jpg`. There is no `ingredients/` subfolder — everything is one level, beside `index.html`.

## How to host it

This is a static site with no build step. Upload the whole folder as one flat directory — `index.html` and every image/video file beside it, no subfolders. If any image lands in a different folder than `index.html`, or in a subfolder, it will fail to load even though the page itself renders fine, since every image path in the HTML is relative to where `index.html` lives.

For GitHub Pages specifically: commit the folder as-is, flat, to the repo (or the branch/folder GitHub Pages is configured to serve from). Do not nest the images into subfolders or rename files — the HTML references exact filenames.

## Version

This package is HoniOra V3 Lite Desktop v109, updated 2026-09-30. Request: revert to v107. This undoes v108's merge of the ingredient-manifesto section (the desktop "Every active, named." static numbered section, built in a separate session and merged in at v108) -- back to v107 exactly, byte-for-byte, with the horizontal `.iband` ingredient photo scroller restored as the desktop-only experience it was through v100-v107. Nothing else changed: the v100-v107 nav swap, hex-drop distance, GLP-1 centering, "Ritual" removal and line-break fixes are all still in place, exactly as v107 shipped them.

What changed:

- **Full revert to v107's own `index.html`, byte-for-byte:** rather than hand-reverting v108's two insertions (the CSS block and the HTML section) piece by piece, this package's `index.html` is v107's own file restored directly from the pristine, unmodified `HoniOra_V3_LiteDesktop_v107.zip`. Confirmed via checksum against that same pristine zip: identical (md5 fca034f7e3abcc37f13493ff3b1404e0).
- **Nothing else touched:** no assets changed (v108 added none), and the rest of the package (every image, video, and the README's own version history below this entry) is otherwise the same as v107.

**Verified.** Checksum-confirmed `index.html` matches the pristine v107 zip exactly. Since this is a byte-for-byte restore of a package already verified at v107, no new Playwright pass was needed beyond that confirmation.

Delivered as `HoniOra_V3_LiteDesktop_v109.zip`.

Prior changes (v107):

This package is HoniOra V3 Lite Desktop v107, updated 2026-09-29. Request: reduce the v106 expanded-hex drop to 45px.

What changed:

- **Expanded-hex vertical offset reduced, 74px to 45px:** `translateY` in the two hex-expand transform rules changed from 74px to the requested 45px, exactly. Nothing else about the rule changed (the horizontal "and in" pull and the doubling itself are unchanged). This is a straight numeric change with a real, disclosed side effect: since both hexes rest with their own top edge flush with the navbar's own top edge at every breakpoint (0px offset, confirmed live), a 45px drop only clears the 42px navbar used in short landscape orientation (3px to spare). It does not clear the 53px navbar (<=560px phones) or the 66px navbar (561px and up, including the main phone/tablet "lite" range and 1001px+ desktop) -- at those widths the expanded hex's top edge again sits inside the navbar's own band, reintroducing the v106 overlap with the burger and the HONIORA title there. Flagging this because it is the same clash v106 was shipped to fix, now back at every width from 375px up to 1000px.

**Verified.** Live checks (a local-GSAP test copy of this package) confirmed the expanded top edge lands at exactly 45px below the resting (0px-offset) position at every tested width, and directly measured the resulting overlap: true (bounding-box intersection with both the burger and the title) at every width from 375px to 1000px, false only at 1001px+ desktop and in short landscape (844x390, 900x414), matching the arithmetic above exactly. A full 22-width overflow sweep from 320px to 1920px found no new overflow anywhere, matching the same pre-existing 320px baseline (355px `scrollWidth`) every version back to v93 has carried.

Delivered as `HoniOra_V3_LiteDesktop_v107.zip`.

Prior changes (v106):

This package is HoniOra V3 Lite Desktop v106, updated 2026-09-29. Request: on the lite site, when the two mini reserve hexes flanking the logo (RESERVE PROTOCOL / RESERVE TEST TUBE) get clicked/tapped and expand, move them down so they don't clash with the burger buttons or the HONIORA title in the nav bar.

What changed:

- **Expanded-hex vertical offset increased, 24px to 74px:** tapping (or hovering) a mini reserve hex doubles its size in place (`scale(2)`) and nudges it down and toward the logo -- the `translateY` half of that nudge was 24px, set at v97. At that offset the expanded hex's top edge only cleared the very top few pixels of the navbar, so its doubled-size body still sat inside the same vertical band as the navbar itself and visibly overlapped the burger button next to it and the centred HONIORA title/logo, confirmed at several widths (measured live, not just visually: at 430px and 480px wide, for example, the expanded hex's bounding box genuinely intersected both the burger's and the title's). Both hexes rest with their own top edge flush with the navbar's own top edge at every breakpoint (confirmed 0px offset live, in both the 66px default lite navbar and the 53px/42px narrower ones), so `translateY` alone controls how far below the navbar the expanded hex lands. Bumped to 74px -- 8px clear of this site's tallest navbar height (66px, used from 561px up through desktop), which also clears the two shorter navbar heights (53px at <=560px phones, 42px in short landscape) by a wider margin. The horizontal "and in" pull (`translateX(14px)`/`translateX(-14px)`) and the doubling itself are unchanged; only the vertical distance moved.

Prior changes (v100):

This package is HoniOra V3 Lite Desktop v100, updated 2026-09-28. Three requests: investigate a report that the v99 tablet roll-in isn't starting fully off-screen and isn't rolling back out on scroll-up; make the whole "A *Natural Active Antipodean Matrix*" heading one uniform gold, not just the italic half; and on the lite site (<=1000px), swap the nav layout so the burgers sit between the hex buttons and the logo instead of the hexes sitting there, with the hex hover/click movement corrected for the new positions.

What changed:

- **Tablet roll-in report, investigated, no code bug found:** re-read the full v99 ScrollTrigger timeline line by line, checked the live deployed site directly (still running a pre-v98 build with no `.tablet-roll` markup at all, so it isn't what was tested), and re-ran the same local-GSAP Playwright checks used to verify v99 originally -- both tablets still measure fully off-screen, flush with the true window edge, before any scroll, and the reverse-on-scroll-up timeline still plays correctly in this test environment. I could not reproduce the reported behaviour and I am not able to say with certainty what caused it. The one change in this package is defensive, not a fix for a confirmed bug: the animation only runs at all when both the GSAP/ScrollTrigger libraries have loaded and the visitor's OS does not have "reduce motion" turned on -- either one being false silently falls back to the tablets' plain CSS resting state (fully visible, already in place, no roll and nothing to reverse), which would look exactly like both symptoms described. Worth checking on the device where this was seen: whether its system-level "Reduce Motion" accessibility setting is on, and whether the browser's console shows either GSAP file failing to load (an ad blocker or content blocker can catch `cdnjs.cloudflare.com`). Nothing was changed in the tablet-roll code itself, since I have no confirmed fault to point at.
- **Heading colour, every breakpoint:** `.unit-head h2` (the "A *Natural Active Antipodean Matrix*" line, appears once in the file) had no `color` of its own, so the plain "A " text inherited the page's default dark ink while only the italic `<em>` portion picked up gold from its own separate rule. Added `color:var(--gold)` to the base `.unit-head h2` rule -- the whole line, "A" included, now renders the same gold as the italic half.
- **Lite-site nav swap, <=1000px only:** the mini gold reserve hexes now sit at the true screen edge and the two burger menu buttons sit between them and the centred wordmark -- the reverse of the previous arrangement. Both switched from grid items to `position:absolute`, with the burger's own inset measured live in JavaScript off the hex's actual rendered width plus a fixed 8px gap, so the two never touch or overlap at any width in this range regardless of how the hex's own clamp-based size renders. Two bugs turned up in testing and were fixed before this shipped: first, an initial version that left the burger anchored inside the grid column produced an ~11.5px real overlap with the hex once the hex left the grid's own flow, caught and fixed by moving the burger to the same live-measured absolute positioning as the hex; second, the hex's existing hover-grow rule (unchanged since it was first built) was tuned for the hex's old position near the logo and grew it further into the header on hover -- correct there, but with the hex now already at the true edge, that same growth pushed it as far as 38px past the left edge of the window. Fixed with a `max-width:1000px`-scoped override flipping the hex's `transform-origin` to grow inward from its own now-outer edge instead, leaving the unscoped base rule (still governing desktop's own hex, which wasn't reported as clipping) untouched. The burger-drop link-list popups were also re-pointed to track the burger's new live position rather than the old fixed offset.
- **Verified:** live Playwright checks (a local-GSAP test copy of this package) at 390px confirmed the new order left to right -- hex, burger, logo, burger, hex -- with an exact 8px gap between each hex and its neighbouring burger regardless of hex width; a real mouse hover on the left hex now measures a positive left edge (no longer clipping off the window, was as far as -38px before the fix); the burger-drop popup opens aligned under its own burger, confirmed both by live rect comparison and a screenshot; desktop's own `>=1001px` nav block (a separate, unscoped set of rules) measured unchanged at 1440px. A full 22-width overflow sweep from 320px to 1920px found no new overflow anywhere -- the one width that does show overflow, 320px (35px), is the same pre-existing baseline every prior version back to v93 has carried, confirmed unrelated to this change by running the identical sweep against the unmodified v99 file, which measures the identical 355px `scrollWidth` at 320px.

Delivered as `HoniOra_V3_LiteDesktop_v100.zip`.

Prior changes (v99):

This package is HoniOra V3 Lite Desktop v99, updated 2026-09-28. Follow-up request on the v98 tablet roll-in: start each tablet flush with the true browser window edge rather than an approximate `vw` offset, slow the animation by 50%, and make it reversible -- scrolling back up should roll the tablets back out to off-screen, not leave them permanently met.

What changed:

- **Reworked reveal mechanism, every breakpoint:** v98's tablet roll-in reused the page's shared `.rv` one-shot scroll-reveal system (a single `IntersectionObserver` that fires once per element and stops watching it), which cannot reverse on scroll-up by design. That's replaced with a dedicated GSAP ScrollTrigger timeline built specifically for `.tablet-roll`: `onEnter` plays the roll-in the first time the block scrolls into view, `onLeaveBack` reverses it (the same timeline played backwards) if the visitor scrolls back up past that same point. The `.rv` class is removed from the wrapper; CSS no longer defines the resting/hidden/transition states for the two tablet images at all -- GSAP now owns their position, rotation, and opacity outright, both at rest and mid-animation.
- **True edge-flush starting position, every breakpoint:** v98 sent each tablet off-screen by a fixed `-70vw`/`+70vw` CSS transform, which is only an approximation of "off-screen" and can under- or overshoot depending on the tablet's actual on-page position and size at a given width. v99 instead measures each tablet's real layout position live, in JavaScript, using `offsetLeft`/`offsetWidth` (values transform never touches) plus its own `offsetParent`'s bounding rect as the coordinate base -- not the `.tablet-roll` wrapper's rect, which turned out to be the wrong reference frame, since the wrapper itself is a plain `position:static` element and the tablets' true `offsetParent` is the enclosing `<section class="sec">` (`position:relative`). Using the wrong base put the right tablet's computed "off-screen" position several hundred pixels inside the visible viewport at 1440px during testing -- caught before shipping, fixed by reading each image's own `offsetParent` instead of assuming it. The result is a starting `translateX` that places each tablet exactly flush with the true window edge (confirmed live: at 1440px the left tablet's right edge sits at `x=0`, the right tablet's left edge at `x=1440`, i.e. `window.innerWidth`), recalculated on resize so it stays accurate at every breakpoint rather than a single fixed guess.
- **Doubled animation duration, every breakpoint:** the roll-in/roll-out transform now runs 2.3s (was 1.15s) and the fade 2s (was 1s), both directions -- reversing a GSAP timeline takes the same duration as playing it forward, so scroll-up satisfies the same 50%-slower request as scroll-down without a separate reverse-specific duration.
- **Packaging bug found and fixed:** while re-testing this change, the `honifizz-tablet-single.jpg` asset added at v98 was found to be missing from the actual v98 package folder on disk -- the HTML/CSS/JS all correctly reference it, but the image file itself never made it into the shipped v98 zip and standalone. This has been corrected: the asset (re-derived from the original upload) is included in this v99 package and confirmed loading with no 404. If the v98 download was used anywhere, the rolling tablets there will show as broken images; this v99 package is the fix.
- **Verified:** live Playwright checks (a local-GSAP test copy of this package) measured both tablets' `getBoundingClientRect()` before any scroll, confirming both sit fully off-screen flush with the true edge at 1440px (`left:-230, right:0` / `left:1440, right:1670`) and at 390px mobile (`left:-122, right:0` / `left:390, right:512`). Scrolling the section into view plays the roll-in, settling both tablets meeting at the centre with the same deliberate overlap as v98; scrolling back up past the trigger reverses the timeline, returning both tablets to their exact original off-screen coordinates. `document.documentElement.scrollWidth` was sampled every 200ms across the full duration of both the forward and reverse transitions at 1440px (12 samples each) -- no overflow at any point, matching the fix already in place from v98. No new console or page errors from the animation itself (the only console entries are a sandboxed-test-environment font fetch, unrelated to this change).

Delivered as `HoniOra_V3_LiteDesktop_v99.zip`.

Prior changes (v98):

This package is HoniOra V3 Lite Desktop v98, updated 2026-09-28. Two requests: replace the static two-tablet product shot at the top of 02 / The Protocol with a scroll-triggered animation of the single tablet photo rolling in from both sides to meet at the centre, and shorten that section's heading.

What changed:

- **Tablet roll-in animation, every breakpoint:** the old `honifizz-tablets.jpg` (one photo already showing two tablets side by side) is replaced by two instances of a new single-tablet photo (`honifizz-tablet-single.jpg`), each starting off-screen -- one past the left edge, one past the right -- rotated a full 540 degrees. The first time this block scrolls into view, both tablets roll inward and spin down to a stop, meeting at the centre with a slight overlap where they touch, exactly where the old static photo used to sit. This reuses the same one-shot scroll-reveal mechanism already built for the lemon slice further down the page (`.rv.lemon-roll`): the reveal fires once, the first time the element crosses into view, and does not reverse or replay on a later scroll back up -- once the tablets meet, they stay met. Two images animating independently off one shared trigger needed one small structural difference from the lemon-roll's single-image version: the trigger class (`.rv`) sits on a wrapper `<div class="tablet-roll">`, with its own opacity/transform reveal turned off (the wrapper itself shouldn't fade-and-rise the way a plain `.rv` block does), while each tablet image carries its own roll-in transform, released together the moment the wrapper gets its `.in` class. The wrapper also carries its own `overflow-x:clip`, matching the same local-clip pattern already used everywhere else on the page that moves an element far outside its own box (`.iband`, `.hrail`) -- relying on `body`'s page-wide clip alone left a brief, real (if easy to miss) window during the pre-reveal and roll-in state where the two off-screen tablets, pulling in opposite directions at once, could register as horizontal overflow.
- **Heading shortened:** "The Depth of a *Natural Active Antipodean Matrix*" is now "A *Natural Active Antipodean Matrix*" -- the rest of the section (the "2 tabs. Every day. It compounds." line, the lede paragraph, and all four protocol phases) is unchanged.
- **Verified:** live Playwright checks (a local-GSAP test copy of this package, served standalone) confirmed the reveal fires once on first scroll into view (`.tablet-roll` gains `.in`), both tablets land at `transform:none, opacity:1`, and they stay revealed after scrolling away and back rather than reversing. Measured both tablets' resting `getBoundingClientRect()` at 1440px: they sit edge to edge with a small deliberate overlap at the centre, matching the old photo's composition. Checked the same at 390px mobile -- same one-shot behaviour, smaller clamp-based size. Full 19-width overflow sweep (320-1920px) -- no new overflow, only the same pre-existing baseline (28px at 561px, 35px at 320px) every prior version has carried; this was specifically re-checked several times over at 1440px after finding and fixing the transient overflow window described above, including a rapid-scroll stress pass, with zero recurrences.

Delivered as `HoniOra_V3_LiteDesktop_v98.zip`.

Prior changes (v95):

This package is HoniOra V3 Lite Desktop v95, updated 2026-09-28. Three requests on the same desktop hero, the first two arriving as a bumped-value follow-up to each other: move the tube, gold fan and ingredient list down 50px (v94), then bumped to 100px before shipping; move the four hero-pillars cards (Pure Origin, Cellular Energy, Vascular Support, Clean Hydration) down 50px too; and slide the pillar icons in 1 second earlier than they had been.

What changed:

- **Tube, gold fan and ingredient list, desktop only (>=1001px), down 100px (was 50px in v94, bumped before that package shipped):** `.hero-actives` (the ingredient list) carries `margin-top:calc(clamp(16px,1.9vh,22px) + 100px)`. Real margin-top, not a transform -- the gap between the list's own bottom and the stats row (`.hero-meta`) directly below it was only 13-39px in the prior layout, so a transform would have visually dropped the list into the stats row. Margin-top instead pushes the list, and every sibling that follows it in the same column -- stats, CTA, the pillars grid -- down together by the same amount, so nothing overlaps. The tube shot needed no CSS change: the hero-keylines script re-centres it, on every rebuild, against the ingredient list's own live-measured vertical midpoint, so it and the gold fan it anchors both followed automatically. The headline/wordmark/tag/born-made copy above the list is untouched.
- **Hero-pillars grid, desktop only (>=1001px), down another 50px:** `.hero-pillars` (the four cards) picked up an extra `50px` on top of its existing `clamp(40px,5vh,72px)` top margin, so its rule now reads `margin-top:calc(clamp(40px,5vh,72px) + 50px)`. This is on top of, not instead of, the 100px the whole row above it already shifted down as a flow-on effect of the ingredient list's own margin change -- the pillars grid's measured top edge moved by 150px total (100px inherited + 50px of its own), which is expected and correct, not a bug: the pillars' own extra margin only had to open 50px more air above the grid than whatever position it would otherwise have landed at. `margin-bottom` was left at its own unchanged `clamp(40px,5vh,72px)` -- only the gap above the grid was asked to grow. Mobile/lite keeps its own separate `margin:18px 0 0` rule (inside the existing `max-width:1000px` block), untouched.
- **Pillar icon slide-in, 1 second earlier:** the icon-reveal `IntersectionObserver` sets each pillar icon's `transitionDelay` to `textDelay + 550 + Nms`, where `N` had been pushed to `2000` by an earlier request (v90) so the icon lands well after its own text. Prior changes (v90):

This package is HoniOra V3 Lite Desktop v90, updated 2026-09-27. Request: increase the font size of all four `.hero-pillars` (Pure Origin, Cellular Energy, Vascular Support, Clean Hydration -- heading and body copy on all four), and delay the four pillar icons' slide-in animation by an additional 2 seconds.

What changed:

- **Font size increased, all four pillars:** `.hero-pillars h3` went from `clamp(18px,1.6vw,23px)` to `clamp(20px,1.85vw,26px)`, and `.hero-pillars p` from `clamp(16px,1.3vw,18.5px)` to `clamp(18px,1.5vw,21px)`. Both rules apply uniformly to all four pillars at every breakpoint (there was no separate mobile override for either), so headings and body copy both read larger everywhere, mobile included.
- **Cascade collision found and fixed along the way:** before this edit, `.hero-pillars h3`/`.hero-pillars p` had no visible effect at all -- a separate, later-in-source rule for the numbered ingredient-section pillars further down the page (`.pillar h3{font-size:23px}`, `.pillar p{font-size:15px}`, from the "pillars"/"pillars4" grid used by sections like Leptosperin and MGO) shares the exact same specificity (one class plus one type selector) and was silently winning on source order, since the hero pillar wrapper divs also carry the bare `.pillar` class (`<div class="pillar" data-edge="l">`) alongside `.hero-pillars` on their ancestor. That's why the hero pillar h3/p had always rendered at a flat 23px/15px regardless of viewport, never the clamp values the CSS appeared to set -- confirmed by testing before this fix that computed font-size was identical (23px/15px) at 390px and 1440px alike. Fixed by rescoping both rules to `.hero-pillars .pillar h3`/`.hero-pillars .pillar p` (two classes plus a type), which outranks the collision by specificity rather than depending on source order. Re-tested after the fix: computed sizes now correctly track the new clamp at every width (e.g. 20px/18px at 1001px and 390px, up to 26px/21px at 1440px and above).
- **Icon slide-in delayed by 2 seconds:** the pillar-icon `IntersectionObserver` handler already staggered each icon's entrance behind its own text ("icon slides in after the copy has landed, not alongside it"), via `icon.style.transitionDelay = (textDelay + 550) + 'ms'`. Changed the fixed offset from `550` to `550 + 2000`, pushing every icon's appearance back an additional two full seconds after its pillar's text has faded in, for all four pillars alike. The text's own fade-in timing (`textDelay`, staggered per pillar's DOM position) is untouched.
- **Verified:** live Playwright check confirmed computed font sizes for `.hero-pillars h3`/`p` now match the new clamp values at 1001/1100/1200/1440/1920px and at 390px mobile (previously stuck at a flat 23px/15px everywhere). Confirmed via direct property read that the first pillar's icon `transitionDelay` computes to 2710ms (160ms text stagger + 550ms original offset + 2000ms new delay), exactly two seconds later than before the fix. Screenshots at 1440px and 390px confirm all four pillars render with the larger text, correct copy, intact `.kw` highlight spans, and no overlap between text and icon at either breakpoint. Full width sweep (320-1920px) -- no new overflow, exactly the same pre-existing overflow already documented at 561px (28px) and 320px (~35px).

Delivered as `HoniOra_V3_LiteDesktop_v90.zip`.

Prior changes (v70):

This package is HoniOra V3 Lite Desktop v70, updated 2026-09-27. Request: on the desktop hero's ingredient list (the flat list fanning out from the tube shot via the gold keylines), move the list left so it centres under "BORN IN NEW ZEALAND > MADE IN USA," and put the "16 / Full dose Natural wellness actives, Zero blends" stat on one line instead of the number stacking above its label.

What changed:

- **Ingredient list centred under the born/made line:** the list's horizontal position was governed entirely by a fixed `--tubeshift` CSS margin (added in an earlier version to fan the gold keylines out wider), which put it well to the right of the born/made line above it rather than centred under it. Added a small JS function, `centerActivesUnderBorn()`, to the existing hero-keylines script, run at the top of its `build()` cycle (so it re-centres on every resize/font-load/orientation-change rebuild that script already listens for, not just once on load). It measures the born/made line's and the ingredient list's real, live horizontal centre and shifts the list to match.
- **Caught and fixed a box-model trap while building this:** the first version of the fix adjusted the list's `margin-left` directly, since that's what the CSS already uses for the existing rightward shift. That silently broke the centering math, because `.hero-actives` is a block-level grid container with no explicit width -- its own rendered width is itself derived from the margin (`width:auto` expands or shrinks to fill whatever the margin doesn't take up), so reducing the margin also grew the list's width by the same amount, leaving it only half-centred. Switched to a `transform:translateX()` shift instead, applied on top of the list's normal CSS position -- transforms don't participate in that width calculation, so the list's width now stays exactly as the surrounding CSS sets it and only its drawn position moves. Confirmed via direct measurement that the list's centre now matches the born/made line's centre to the sub-pixel at every width tested (1001-1920px), with the list's own width unchanged from before the fix at every one of those widths.
- **Desktop only:** scoped to the same `>=1001px` range as every other hero-actives positioning rule, using the script's existing `mqLite` check. Mobile/lite (<=1000px) keeps its own plain, unshifted list position, untouched.
- **Hero stat, one line:** `.hero-meta b` ("16") has always been `display:block`, stacking it above its `<span>` label at every breakpoint -- deliberately kept for mobile, which centres the stat as two lines (per an earlier v62 request), but with no equivalent desktop reason to keep it stacked. Added a desktop-only rule (`>=1001px`) making `.hero-meta div` a flex row (`align-items:baseline`, a 10px gap) so the number and label sit side by side on shared line instead. At most desktop widths this fits as one full line; in the narrower 1001-1300px range the label text itself can still wrap onto a second line for lack of horizontal room, but the number no longer sits stacked alone above it either way. Mobile's own stacked, centred treatment is untouched.
- **Verified:** live Playwright check confirmed the ingredient list's centre matches the born/made line's centre to the sub-pixel at seven widths from 1001px to 1920px, and confirmed the mobile list keeps `transform:none` and the mobile stat keeps its stacked `display:block` layout untouched. Screenshots at 1920px confirm both changes visually. Full width sweep (320-1920px) -- no new overflow, only the same pre-existing overflow already documented at 561px (28px) and 320px (~35px), both unrelated to this change.
  - Delivered as `HoniOra_V3_LiteDesktop_v70.zip`.

Requested (across several follow-up messages after v56 shipped): "REVERT THE MAIN SITE BUT IMPLEMENT THIS SCROLL ON THE LITE SITE ONLY" / "THE MAIN SITE IS DISPLAYING THE VERITCAL SCROLL AND TH ELITE ISNT (SWAP THIS OVER)" / "IT DELIVERS THE MAIN SITE AND LITE FOR MOBILES" -- i.e. swap which breakpoint gets the vertical ingredient tumbler.

What this actually was: v56 built the tumbler for the desktop pinned-scroll mode (>=1001px) per that version's own two confirmed clarifying answers at the time. Once it was live, it became clear the user's own "main" / "lite" naming doesn't refer to two separately deployed packages (this branch versus the original-brand `HONIORA_site_vNNN` line) -- it refers to responsive breakpoints within this one package: "main" = whatever renders at desktop widths, "lite" = whatever renders at mobile widths (<=1000px), of this single live site at `https://gregmillerhard.github.io/HoniOra/`. So v56 had the tumbler on the wrong side of that breakpoint. v57 swapped it -- and then, per the request that produced this v58 package, the whole tumbler idea was dropped and the band reverted to v55 entirely.

Recent changes in this version:

- The ingredient image band (`.iband`) went back to its original desktop behaviour: a full-width horizontal strip the pinned scroll pans left-to-right, off the same 0-1 progress as the claims ticker below it -- byte-identical to how it rendered before v56 (confirmed against a fresh unzip of the pre-v56 `HoniOra_V3_LiteDesktop_v55.zip`, since the working v55 folder had itself been overwritten mid-session and could no longer be trusted as the revert source). Per-thumbnail captions (`.icap`) went back to their original fade-in-on-focus behaviour too, and the `.itag` title/copy bar from v56 no longer rendered on desktop at all.
- The vertical tumbler + shared `.itag` title/dose/copy bar moved to the mobile/"lite" breakpoint (<=1000px) instead, as an addition layered on top of the existing `.unit.static` mobile mode rather than a replacement of it. A `.iband-tumbler` modifier class (added only by the working-GSAP mobile branch, never by the true no-GSAP/reduced-motion `fallback()`) distinguished "mobile with the tumbler" from "true flat-grid safe fallback" -- both of which still carried `.static` -- so every one of the flat-grid CSS rules (`.unit.static .iband{...}`, `.ishot{...}`, the pre-anim slide-in reveal, the narrow-screen two-up card tweaks, etc.) was rescoped to `.unit.static:not(.iband-tumbler)`, and the tumbler rules used the compound selector `.unit.static.iband-tumbler` instead.
- The tumbler got its own independent `ScrollTrigger.create({trigger: iband, pin:true, ...})` pin on mobile, scoped to `.iband` alone -- not the whole `.unit` section -- so it never touched the claims ticker's separate, pre-existing mobile behaviour (plain stacked list, `.upanel.active` highlight via IntersectionObserver).
- `markFocus()` was made orientation-agnostic: it measured 2D distance (`Math.sqrt(dx*dx + dy*dy)`) from `.iband-vp`'s own centre, rather than a single-axis measurement, so one function served both the horizontal desktop band and the vertical mobile tumbler.
- Fixed a specificity conflict the swap would otherwise have hit (the static-fallback `!important` transform rule) and a separate pre-anim slide-in script conflict, both rescoped/gated to exclude `.iband-tumbler`.
- **Verified at the time:** Playwright at 1440x900 and 390x844 confirmed the desktop/mobile split worked as intended, and the claims ticker was unaffected.
  - Delivered as `HoniOra_V3_LiteDesktop_v57.zip`.

Prior changes (v53):

- The user supplied a screenshot of the OXYLIFE wordmark as a font reference: a Didone/Bodoni-style serif with extreme thick/thin stroke contrast, fine hairline serifs and tall, elegant proportions. That exact typeface isn't identifiable as a font available to license or load from a font CDN (it reads as either a custom cut or a tightly-licensed commercial face), so the closest available match was used instead: Bodoni Moda, a free Google Fonts release built specifically as a high-contrast Bodoni revival. Added to the page's existing Google Fonts `<link>` (alongside Inter, Playfair Display and Cinzel, already loaded the same way), requesting weights 400 and 500 in both roman and italic.
- Scope: only the hero's big "HONIORA" wordmark (`#heroWordmark`, `.hero-logo`) uses live, restyleable text. `.hero-logo` changed from Playfair Display at weight 700 to Bodoni Moda at weight 400 (with `font-optical-sizing:auto` so the typeface's built-in optical-size axis adjusts hairline thickness correctly at this display size), and the "ORA" span's weight eased from 600 to 500 italic to keep it visually in the same family as the roman "HONI" half rather than reading bold-on-regular. Letter-spacing nudged from .005em to .02em, closer to the airier spacing in the reference logo.
- Explicitly NOT changed: the nav-bar and footer "HONIORA" wordmark is a raster image (`honiora-logo.png`), not text, so a font-family change has no effect on it. Restyling that would mean regenerating the logo image itself in the new typeface, a separate design task, not attempted here since it wasn't part of what was asked and needs its own review before replacing a brand asset. Every other serif use on the page (`.serif` class on section headings like "About HoniCo", "The Company") is unrelated to the HoniOra wordmark and was left on its existing font.
- **Verified:** confirmed `#heroWordmark` computes `font-family` to `"Bodoni Moda", Georgia, "Times New Roman", serif` at both 1440px and 390px, and a full-width overflow check at both breakpoints came back at 0px on this package's own two branches. This sandbox's outbound network policy blocked the live Google Fonts CDN fetch during this session's own verification pass, the same CDN the site's other three typefaces already depend on in production, so the rendered glyphs themselves couldn't be screenshotted here on this pass -- confirmed instead against a standalone Bodoni Moda render made earlier in this session, which matched the reference image's hairline-serif, high-contrast letterforms closely.
  - Delivered as `HoniOra_V3_LiteDesktop_v53.zip`.

Prior changes (v51):

That gap widens fast as the viewport gets wider (roughly 5px at 1041px, ~130px at 1440px, ~270px at 1920px), so it can't be closed with one fixed offset without either barely moving the copy at wide widths or crashing it into the video at narrow ones. Added four extra breakpoints (1241px, 1401px, 1601px, 1801px) that increase `.hero-copy-shift`'s existing leftward `translateX` in steps (-90px, -130px, -180px, -240px, up from the base -50px used below 1241px), each tuned to leave a ~35-40px safety margin against the video's own real rendered edge at that bracket's narrowest point -- verified with Playwright by measuring the actual video element's bounding box, not just its column.
- Only `.hero-copy-shift` (the headline/tag/born-made block) moves. The ingredient actives list, dose stats, and CTA buttons stay exactly where they were -- the gold connector-line fan measures the list's live position and wasn't part of what was asked to move.
- **Verified:** measured the gap between the copy block's shifted left edge and the video's real rendered right edge at sixteen widths from 1024px to 1920px (including both sides of every new breakpoint) -- positive clearance (28px or more) at every one, zero overlap. Confirmed the copy block's horizontal centre moved meaningfully closer to the page's own centre at every width tested (e.g. at 1440px, the gap to page-centre shrank from 275px to 196px; at 1920px, from 426px to 236px -- a partial move, not a perfect centering, since the video column's own width wasn't touched). A full sweep of all fifteen standard widths (1920px down to 320px) confirmed zero new horizontal overflow -- the same two pre-existing, already-documented quirks (28px at 561px, 35px at 320px) are the only ones present.

Recent changes in this version:

- Request: "add some of this to section 10 / Pomma+ & Urolithin A," supplying a marketing-style paragraph about punicalagins (their size as a polyphenol molecule, where in the fruit they occur, their antioxidant role, and ongoing research into heart, cognitive, and gut-microbiome effects). Rather than pasting it in as supplied, rewrote it in the page's own established voice for this section, plain and cited rather than metaphor-heavy ("ruby depths," "nature's gift," "personal guard" and similar were dropped), since every other claim in this section carries a citation and the supplied text carried none. Checked the standout factual claim, that punicalagins rank among the largest known polyphenol molecules, against an outside source before using it (Tyagi et al., a pomegranate/punicalagin review republished by NC State's Horticulture International, which independently supports it) rather than taking the supplied wording on faith. Added it as a second `.lede` paragraph directly under the section's existing intro line, with its own citation link in the same style as the rest of the page; added a small scoped CSS rule (`#pomma .sec-head .lede+.lede{margin-top:16px}`) so the two stacked paragraphs get a visible gap between them, mirroring the identical rule already used for the same two-paragraph pattern in the "05 / Creatine" section. Left the softer, uncited claim about punicalagins reducing "complications of aging" out, since nothing in the supplied text or the source checked actually backs a specific aging claim and the section's own house rule is not to state anything past what its citation supports. Verified with Playwright that the section now renders both paragraphs with working citation links and no console errors, and a screenshot confirms the spacing and layout read cleanly.
  - Delivered as part of `HoniOra_V3_LiteDesktop_v48.zip`.

- Request: "put a big gap at the front of the first scroller make the first line load in the middle of the page," referring to the hero marquee ticker (the "1st scroll bar," `#railTrack`). Added a new `.hrail-gap` spacer div as the first child inside `.hrail-set`, sized `clamp(160px,18vw,340px)`, so it lives inside the set that gets cloned for the seamless loop and the gap repeats every cycle automatically rather than only showing once. Adding the gap broke the ticker's existing open-centred-on-load behaviour, since the centring formula assumed the first message was the track's own first child with zero lead-in distance; fixed `centerFirst()` to measure the actual gap between the message's and the track's own `getBoundingClientRect().left` values instead of assuming zero, which stays correct at any viewport width and doesn't depend on which element the browser treats as the offset parent. Hit a false alarm partway through: an initial fix using `element.offsetLeft` measured wrong (`offsetLeft` resolves against the nearest positioned ancestor, `.hrail`, not the track itself), and after correcting that, injected debug logging appeared to show the centred position drifting away within the first second of every test run, as if the intro's static hold was being skipped. Traced that to a test-harness artifact, not a real bug: my verification script was waiting for Playwright's `networkidle` before sampling, and this page's blocked Google Fonts requests (a pre-existing sandbox network restriction, unrelated to this change) hang long enough that `networkidle` can resolve several seconds late, by which point the real 8-second hold had already finished silently in the background, so the first sample was already catching the tail end of the loop rather than the actual start. Re-verified waiting on `load` instead: the first message now renders exactly centred at 0px off true centre the instant the page loads, holds perfectly static through the full 8-second pause, eases into the loop right on cue via the existing 1.1s handoff transition, and drifts forward smoothly afterward with no jump, all with the gap intact on every repeat.
  - Delivered as part of `HoniOra_V3_LiteDesktop_v48.zip`.

- Request: "add this as article 02 of The HoniBlog [@OptimalHealthLab25](https://www.youtube.com/@OptimalHealthLab25/shorts) — Brian Johnson: Extra Virgin Olive Oil is Better Than Ozempic https://youtube.com/shorts/SZS4lVGoz6I?si=cYpdmy_GfhhZ4uKm," with a screenshot of the video attached as the thumbnail. Article 02 was already taken (Dr Stacy Sims' Rhodiola piece), so I asked whether this should land as the newest post instead (Article 07) or genuinely take the 02 slot with everything else renumbered up; the answer was to renumber. Article 02 (Rhodiola) through Article 06 (Mānuka Honey) each moved up one number, this new entry took the 02 slot second from the bottom, directly above Article 01 ("Six habits for healthy ageing"), and Article 01 itself was untouched. The attached screenshot was saved as `blog-olive-oil-ozempic.jpg` (1262×2262, matching its exact source pixel dimensions) and wired in with the same markup pattern every other video-still article uses: a `data-yt` id, the still with descriptive alt text, and a play-button link out to the actual short. Tagged the entry "Ingredients," since HONIORA already carries olive polyphenols in its own formula (Hytolive Bev Olive Extract, the Heart-system ingredient behind the EFSA blood-lipid claim in the Stack section), the same basis the other ingredient-tie-in articles (Manuka, Rhodiola, Pomegranate, Creatine) are tagged on. Corrected the presenter's name in the title and alt text from "Brian" to "Bryan" — Bryan Johnson is a real, identifiable public figure (the Blueprint/"Don't Die" longevity entrepreneur), and a web search confirms that spelling and that the video's premise is a real, widely-reported comment of his (he told his own podcast "Extra virgin olive oil is... better than Ozempic," a claim he later said was made partly "being cheeky," grouping olive oil with several other interventions rather than a strict clinical equivalence claim). Flagging that for the record since this article, like the others in this stack, is just a title and a thumbnail linking out to the creator's own video — HONIORA isn't asserting the Ozempic comparison itself anywhere on the page, only captioning and linking to a real third-party short, the same treatment every other HoniBlog entry gets. Verified with Playwright that all seven articles now render in 07→01 order with correct titles, image sources, alt text, and outbound YouTube links, and that the new image loads with no console errors introduced.
  - Delivered as part of `HoniOra_V3_LiteDesktop_v48.zip`.

- Request: "on the mobile (lite) check that the glass video is displaying dead center under the HoniOra logo in the nav bar at top." It was not: measured both elements' true rendered centres with Playwright at five widths (360-1000px) and found the video sitting consistently var(--pad)/2 left of the logo's centre (13px at 360-430px, up to 25px at 1000px, tracking half of the `--pad` custom property almost exactly at every width tested). Root cause: `.hero-lower` (the row holding the video, the marquee ticker, and the tube shot on mobile) carries `padding-right:var(--pad)` but no matching `padding-left` at all, so its content box, and `.hero-banner` stretched to fill it, sit flush against the left edge with the "missing" width left over on the right instead -- a leftover from the desktop layout, where that lopsided padding deliberately reserves space for the image column, that was never rebalanced for mobile's single-column stack. The nav logo is centred by the separate lite-nav layout, so it doesn't inherit this lean and was already sitting dead centre; only the hero content below it was off. Fixed by adding `margin-left:calc(var(--pad) / 2)` to `.hero-banner` alone, rather than touching the shared `.hero-lower` padding that the marquee ticker's full-bleed formula and the tube-shot row are both separately tuned against, so this doesn't risk shifting either of those. Verified with Playwright at the same five widths: the video's rendered centre now matches the logo's to within 0.01px at every one, and a 390px screenshot confirms the glass sits visually centred under the wordmark.
  - Delivered as part of `HoniOra_V3_LiteDesktop_v48.zip`.

The Ask Honi widget's `ahAsk()` function previously appended every question and answer onto the end of the thread, so a long session built up a scrolling stack of every past exchange (plus the opening greeting) still sitting above the newest one. `ahAsk()` now clears the thread (`athread.innerHTML = ''`) before adding the question just asked, whether it came from typing and submitting or from clicking one of the starter chips, so only the current question and its answer are ever shown -- the opening greeting and every prior exchange are cleared out the moment a new question comes in. This is the ikigai diagram's one-time scroll-triggered intro: the first time the four-pillar diagram scrolls into view, its four rings build their gold colour on their own, one at a time in a random order, hold for a beat, then clear back to the plain outline. That sequence had already been doubled once before, from an original 300ms per-ring step / 1400ms hold to 600ms / 2800ms. Doubled again, to 1200ms per ring / 5600ms hold -- the full build-then-reset now takes four times as long as the original to play out. Only the two timing constants changed; the random shuffle order, the hover-to-build behaviour, and the reduced-motion skip are untouched.

- Request: "check that the specs in the site body copy and scrollers is accurate based on the latest MBR", followed by an uploaded MBR PDF (`HoniOra Master Batch Record 1.pdf`, document ID `MBR-HNR-PLUS-V8.1`). Built a canonical 29-line reference table from the project's MBR history (the currently-adopted formula: v7.9.1 plus the Pomma+ addition -- 5,724.50 mg/tab, 11,449.00 mg/serving, 29 total bill-of-materials lines), had a subagent cross-check every dose and weight figure in the page's body copy, hero ticker, stack-matrix table, AskHoni answers and legal copy against it, then verified the canonical table itself against the newly-uploaded PDF once it arrived -- the PDF's own 29-line BOM matches the table exactly line for line (same 1,500 mg Cr-01 Creatine per serving, same 750 mg/tab combined MGO 850+/Fiberest HF line, same 250 mg/tab Pomma+, same 5,724.50 mg/5,890.67 mg nominal/gross tablet totals), confirming it as the same adopted formula rather than a newer revision. Three real discrepancies found and fixed, one initial misread caught and reverted:
  1. The Unit-section line "HONIORA tablets each contain 5.4745 grams of full dose potent actives" was the pre-Pomma+ tablet weight, stale since Pomma+ was added (this exact staleness had been flagged-but-deliberately-left-unfixed in an earlier version's notes, pending a decision). Now reads 5.7245 grams, matching the MBR's confirmed total.
  2. Initially misread as an error, then reverted once the user supplied the missing context: the Mānuka Honey nav-dropdown's "1,000 mg of MGO 850+ / UMF 20+ Mānuka Honey Crystals" and the Mānuka Honey section's own lede ("delivers 1,000 mg... It is a 1,500 mg high potency crystal biobase") both cite 1,000 mg, while five other site locations (hero ticker, stack-matrix table, lemon-comparison table, AskHoni, and more) cite 1,500 mg for "MGO 850+ Mānuka / Fiberest HF" as a combined line. The MBR's BOM only gives one combined figure (750 mg/tab, 1,500 mg/serving) with no Manuka-only sub-breakdown, so this was first (wrongly) treated as an inconsistency and both 1,000 mg instances were changed to 1,500 mg. The user then supplied the missing context directly: of the 1,500 mg combined MGO 850+ Manuka/Fiberest HF blend, 1,000 mg specifically is Manuka honey crystals, the rest is the Fiberest HF fibre matrix. Both edits were reverted back to their original, correct wording: the nav-dropdown and the Mānuka Honey lede both correctly cite 1,000 mg (the honey-only component), while every other site location correctly cites 1,500 mg (the combined blend) -- no actual error existed at either of these two locations. Lesson: a number that looks inconsistent against a BOM's single combined line isn't necessarily wrong, if the BOM doesn't break out a sub-component the site is legitimately citing.
  3. A derived "27%" stat ("Cr-01 Creatine is... a little over a quarter of the entire compression blend by weight") was computed pre-Pomma+ (3,000 mg / 10,949 mg) and never recalculated once Pomma+ raised the per-serving total to 11,449 mg. Corrected to 26% (3,000 / 11,449 = 26.2%), with the caption's "a little over a quarter" softened to "roughly a quarter" to match.
  4. The "Copyright and IP" trademark list was missing Eriomin, Hytolive and Pomma+ -- all three are real branded ingredients in the current formula, and all three were already correctly listed in the separate footer trademark disclaimer just below it. Added to bring the two lists back in sync.
  - Confirmed clean by the same audit (no change needed): the 29-row stack-matrix table, hero ticker, hero-actives list, hero stat card, ishot captions, Protocol timeline, ikigai/triad quadrant lists, meta description, and all AskHoni knowledge-base ingredient entries.
  - Two items noted but not changed, since they're judgement calls rather than clear-cut errors: the footer's batch-record identifier reads `MBR-MN-HONIORA-V7.9.1-140K-25MM`, while the newly-uploaded PDF's own document ID is `MBR-HNR-PLUS-V8.1` -- the dose data in both matches exactly, so this looks like an internal document renumbering rather than a formula change, but the on-site identifier hasn't been updated to match; and the site describes the formula as "29 named actives," though the MBR's own BOM splits those 29 lines into 18 true active ingredients plus 11 excipients/buffers/lubricants/flavours (citric acid, sodium bicarbonate, PEG 6000, and so on) -- left as-is since this phrasing predates this audit and spans many earlier revisions, but worth a decision on which framing is intended.
  - Verified with Playwright: all three corrected figures render correctly with no console errors, the reverted Mānuka Honey wording renders exactly as it did before this audit touched it, and the two now-matching trademark lists were diffed directly.
  - Delivered as part of `HoniOra_V3_LiteDesktop_v48.zip`.

- Bug report: "the lite version is still displaying the desktop sunrise scene", then "remove the sunrise video from the lite site." The hero video used to be a single `<video>` element with two `<source>` tiers, picked by the browser via each `<source>`'s own `media` attribute (mobile's first, desktop's golden-hour footage as the fallback). That's a real browser-spec limitation for exactly this situation: a `<video>`'s `<source media>` list is only evaluated ONCE, when the element first parses/loads -- resizing the browser afterward, or switching a device-preview tool from desktop to mobile width without a full page reload, does not make it re-pick sources on its own. A page first loaded at a desktop width kept playing the golden-hour video even once sized down to mobile, which is what "still displaying" described.
  1. Split into two separate `<video>` elements (`#heroGlassVideoMobile`, `#heroGlassVideoDesktop`), the same duplicate-and-toggle pattern this version already uses for the wax-seal medallion. Each one's `<source>` list now contains ONLY its own footage -- the mobile element has zero reference to the golden-hour assets anywhere in its markup, and (no `autoplay` attribute, `preload="none"`) its browser has no reason to ever fetch that footage in the first place, confirmed via a network-request capture during a pure mobile session: zero requests for the golden-hour video files.
  2. Which one is visible is pure CSS, toggled at the same 1001px breakpoint as everything else in this hero. First shipped this as a single-class rule (`.hero-glass-video-desktop{display:none}`), which turned out to lose a CSS specificity fight against `.hero-glass-img`'s own existing `display:block` rules (two classes deep) regardless of source order -- confirmed via Playwright that the desktop video stayed `display:block` under the mobile one even though JS had correctly paused it, meaning a blank or frozen video box would still have sat there. Fixed by writing the toggle three selectors deep (`.hero-banner .hero-glass-img.hero-glass-video-desktop`) so it always wins.
  3. A small script keeps exactly one of the two videos actually playing, matching whichever CSS is currently showing, and re-runs on resize/orientationchange -- so a live resize across the 1000px line without a reload (the original bug's root cause) is now handled correctly instead of leaving the wrong one running underneath.
  - Verified with Playwright: a fresh load at mobile width, a fresh load at desktop width, and a live resize crossing 1000px in both directions (no reload) all show the correct video with the correct one paused and the correct one playing every time; a mobile session's network log shows no requests at all for the golden-hour video files; desktop still shows the golden-hour video correctly with no console errors.
  - Delivered as part of `HoniOra_V3_LiteDesktop_v48.zip`.

- Request: pasted copy for the Pomma+ & Urolithin A section (body paragraph and Selma et al. 2014 citation), asking for the section to be updated. The body paragraph and citation link the copy specified were already an exact match for what's on the page (`<h2>`'s companion `<p class="lede">` and its `Supporting research: Selma et al. 2014` link, section 10, "Pomma+ & Urolithin A") -- only the headline itself differed. Updated the `<h2>` from "Pomma+ Pomegranate: the precursor, not the shortcut." to "Pomma+ Pomegranate: Urolithin A precursor", matching the wording supplied. Verified with Playwright/screenshot that the section renders correctly with no layout issues.
  - Delivered as part of `HoniOra_V3_LiteDesktop_v48.zip`.

- Bug report (screenshots showing "MĀNUKA CREATINE PROTOCOL" and "BORN IN NEW ZEALAND > MADE IN USA" running past the card's edge): "fix the text spill outside the box, either make the box bigger or the text smaller." Two real bugs in the page's existing shrink-to-fit scripts, both pre-dating this session but only ever exposed once the golden-hour hero's overlay card (this version's own new addition) became much narrower than the old side-by-side desktop layout's text column ever was:
  1. `.hero-tag` ("MĀNUKA CREATINE PROTOCOL") had NO overflow safety net at all above 1400px -- `matchHeroTagWidth()` only ever matched its font-size to the rendered width of the HONIORA wordmark above it, with nothing checking that result against the actual card it sits in. Below 1400px it had no JS sizing at all, just a plain CSS `min(2.6vw,36px)` rule written for the old, much wider column. Added a real safety-net check (unconditional, not just above 1400px) that measures the tag's true rendered width and shrinks it further if it doesn't fit the card.
  2. `#bornMadeLine` ("Born in New Zealand > Made in USA") DID have a safety-net check already (`matchLinesToTagWidth()`), but the check itself was broken: it measured `el.getBoundingClientRect().width`, which for a block-level `<p>` always reports the box's assigned width, not the true width of its `white-space:nowrap` text -- a block box doesn't shrink-wrap to its content, so the check could never actually detect its own overflow. Switched to checking `el.scrollWidth` instead, which does reflect the true content width for a nowrap block. That fix alone then broke the OTHER line this same function also guards, `#precedeFit` ("2 effervescent tablets & 1 glass of water & 60 sec.") -- it's a plain inline `<span>`, and genuinely-inline elements report `scrollWidth` as 0 in this browser regardless of overflow, the opposite failure mode. Final fix takes `Math.max(el.scrollWidth, el.getBoundingClientRect().width)`, which is correct for both element types at once (whichever metric is "wrong" for a given element under-reports rather than over-reports, so the larger of the two is always the true rendered width).
  - Verified with Playwright at 1001/1024/1100/1200/1300/1400/1440/1500/1600/1680/1750/1800/1920px: an automated sweep of every text node inside the card confirms zero elements render wider than their own available box at any of those widths (previously multiple lines overflowed by 20-150+ px at every single width tested, including the narrowest); all three affected lines (`.hero-tag`, `#bornMadeLine`, `#precedeFit`) now scale smoothly with viewport width with no jumps; vertical (top/bottom) clearance against the video box actually improved slightly at every width, since correctly-shrunk text makes the card a little shorter too; mobile (390px) unaffected, confirmed via a separate pass with no console errors.
  - Delivered as part of `HoniOra_V3_LiteDesktop_v48.zip`.

- Request: "make the text box on screen 1 wider and move it right 50px and up 75px." Desktop only (1001px+), the golden-hour hero's overlay copy card, on top of the widen-and-recentre change below it:
  1. Widened further above ~1400px: the extra-width term went from `(100vw - 1400px) * 0.4` to `* 0.6` and the ceiling from 640px to 720px, so the card now reaches ~720px by 1920px (versus ~640px before this request). The untouched-below-1400px behaviour stays the same, for the same clipping-avoidance reason as before.
  2. Added a literal pixel offset on top of the existing 58%/50% centring point: `translate(calc(-50% + 50px), calc(-50% - <up amount>))`, so it moves 50px right at every desktop width (huge horizontal margin either side of the card at every width tested, confirmed safe via Playwright) and up by an amount that ramps from 0px at 1400px to the full 75px by ~1920px (`clamp(0px, (100vw - 1400px) * 0.12, 75px)`), rather than a flat 75px everywhere.
  3. The upward shift specifically had to be tapered rather than flat: a flat 75px-up shift clipped "UPGRADE" against `.hero-banner`'s `overflow:hidden` top edge by 70-80px at 1024-1200px, confirmed via Playwright before this was changed -- at those widths the aspect-ratio-locked banner is already short enough that the vertically-centred card has almost no spare margin to give up. Below 1400px the card stays exactly vertically centred, same as before this request; from 1400px up it lifts progressively, reaching the full 75px by 1920px with a few pixels of margin still spare.
  4. Verified with Playwright at 1001/1024/1200/1440/1680/1920px: no top/bottom overflow introduced at any width beyond the same handful-of-pixels baseline that already existed at 1024-1200px before this request (unrelated to this change, see the widen-and-recentre entry below), the card sits visibly right-shifted and wider at 1440px+, and mobile (390px) is unaffected since this rule lives entirely inside the existing 1001px+ desktop block.
  - Delivered as part of `HoniOra_V3_LiteDesktop_v48.zip`.

Widened at every desktop width above ~1400px: the card's own width formula added an extra term on top of the existing `38vw - 130px` base, capped at a raised ceiling (both later increased again by the request above). Below 1400px the formula is untouched -- same width as before at 1024-1400px -- specifically because that narrower range was already right at the edge of a clipping bug (see the golden-hero-video entry below): widening it further there reintroduced the same top/bottom clipping against `.hero-banner`'s `overflow:hidden`, confirmed via Playwright before this was ruled out in favor of only widening where there's headroom to spare.
  3. Verified with Playwright at 1024/1200/1440/1680/1920px: the card's rendered width increases progressively above 1400px with no overflow against the video box at any of those widths (same small pre-existing few-pixel margin as before at 1024-1200px, comfortably clear at 1440px+), the card's horizontal centre sits at 58% of the banner's width at every size, and mobile (390px) is unaffected since this rule lives entirely inside the existing 1001px+ desktop block.

- Request: "move the wax seal and circle of words to always cover the gemini watermark no matter what screen size." The golden-hour clip is AI-generated stock footage carrying a small sparkle/star watermark baked into the footage itself. Measured its exact position directly off the poster image and two separate video frames (frame 0 and frame 120, to confirm it doesn't drift): a fixed 90% across, 88.5% down the 1280x640 frame, every time. Since `.hero-banner`'s aspect-ratio is locked to that same 1280/640 ratio, `object-fit:cover` never actually crops anything, so that fractional position holds exactly at every viewport width. Added a second, separate wax-seal medallion (`.hero-seal-onvideo`, a duplicate of the existing one -- same markup, its inner SVG id suffixed "Video" to stay unique on the page) positioned with matching left/top percentages plus its own `translate(-50%,-50%)` to centre ON that point, so it tracks the watermark automatically rather than needing a hand-tuned position per breakpoint. Desktop only: the original medallion (still just below the marquee, inside `.hrail`, completely unchanged) is hidden at 1001px+ and this new one takes over there instead; mobile is untouched since its studio glass-on-white video has no watermark to cover. Verified with Playwright at 1024/1200/1440/1920px that the new medallion's measured centre matches the expected `bannerLeft + bannerWidth*0.90, bannerTop + bannerHeight*0.885` point exactly, and that mobile (390/800/991px) still shows only the original medallion in its original spot.

- Request: "make the desktop version look like this [reference image: golden-hour lifestyle photo, glass + two tablets on a wood table, with an overlay copy card and wax-seal medallion] with all the new attributes and the burger nav bar, make the lite version have the older glass on white video with all the new attributes." Desktop (1001px+) only:

The video itself switches from the portrait glass-on-white studio shot to this branch's own existing golden-hour lifestyle footage (`hero-gold-loop.mp4`/`.webm`, present in this package since before v45 but unused on desktop since then), center-cropped to the new 1280x640 ratio -- the crop files (`hero-gold-loop-crop.mp4`/`.webm`, `hero-gold-loop-poster-crop.jpg`) were copied in from the vNNN line's own identical assets rather than re-derived, since it's the same source footage either way. A small inline script swaps in the correct landscape poster for desktop only (mobile keeps its own portrait poster), the same synchronous-swap pattern this branch's own v38 fix and vNNN's own hero both already use, since the `poster` attribute can't be conditioned on a media query the way a `<source>` can.
  3. This deliberately reverses v45's removal of the golden-hour video for desktop -- v45's own reasoning doesn't apply any more (it was written when desktop still played this branch's own portrait video, which really would look absurd full-bleed; desktop no longer plays that video at all). The burger nav bar is unaffected either way -- this branch has shown it at every width, including desktop, since it became the permanent default nav (see the top of this README); nothing about this change touches it.

Mobile/lite (<=1000px) is completely untouched, per the user's own confirmation -- still the boxed portrait glass-on-white video exactly as v45 shipped it, with all of this session's other additions (Pomma+, the marquee edits, the new Urolithin A section, etc.) already present there too, since those apply site-wide rather than being hero-specific.

Bug found and fixed during verification, not present in the reference site's own treatment: below roughly 1450px, the full-bleed banner's aspect-ratio-locked height shrinks faster than the overlay card's own width-driven text sizing does, so the card ended up taller than the video box beneath it, and `.hero-banner`'s own `overflow:hidden` clipped the top and bottom of the headline against that edge (the top of "UPGRADE" disappeared behind the nav bar at 1024-1200px). Confirmed the identical clipping happens on the vNNN line's own live hero-banner at the same widths, so this wasn't introduced here -- just not worth carrying over, especially since the whole point of this change is to look right at real desktop widths, not just the wide screenshot the reference image happened to be taken at. Fixed with a single continuous width formula on the copy card (`clamp(230px, calc(38vw - 130px), 560px)` in place of vNNN's flat `clamp(340px,38vw,560px)`) that keeps the same result at wide widths (both already hit the same 560px ceiling there) while continuously narrowing the card -- and, via the existing shrink-to-fit scripts that already measure the card's real rendered width on every resize, its text -- as the viewport (and the banner's own height with it) gets smaller. No hard breakpoint, so no visible jump in card size at any particular width.

Verified with Playwright at 1024/1200/1440/1600/1920px: the video source, poster, and full-bleed sizing are all correct at every desktop width; the overlay card no longer overflows the video box anywhere in that range (previously clipped by 36-38px top and bottom at 1024-1200px); mobile widths (390/800/991px) confirmed still on the unchanged portrait glass-on-white video in the original contained layout; the burger nav popup still opens correctly with no console errors; full overflow sweep across all ten widths shows zero new horizontal overflow (the same pre-existing 320px marquee-track quirk as every prior version).

This also brought the package back under the delivery size limit after adding the new crop assets.

- Request: "move the ingredient list right 150px and center the tube half way down the list so that the gold lines progressively fan across in an elegant symmetrical fashion." Two changes, desktop only (1001px+, the width range this tube+list side-by-side row exists at all):
  1. The ingredient list (`.hero-actives`) now sits further right of the tube, widening the horizontal spread the gold connector lines fan across. The stats row and CTA buttons below the list were deliberately left at their original position -- only the list itself moves, matching what was asked.
  2. The tube image's vertical position is now actively centered, via a small addition to the existing hero-keylines script, against the true vertical midpoint of the ingredient list itself (the span between the first and last ingredient's own tick), rather than the CSS auto-margin centering already in place, which centered it against the taller box that also includes the stats row and CTA buttons below the list. That meant the tube previously sat below the list's own midpoint, so every gold line fanned mostly downward rather than spreading evenly above and below. The correction is applied as a `transform:translateY(...)` on the tube image itself (recomputed on every geometry rebuild the script already performs -- resize, font load, scroll-reveal, etc.), never a layout change, so nothing else in the row shifts.
  - The 150px list shift is fluid, not flat, specifically to avoid a regression at the narrow end of this breakpoint: `.hero-lower` reserves a fixed 430px on its right for the wax-seal medallion, which eats a much bigger share of the row at ~1024px than at 1920px. A flat 150px shift was tested first and made every ingredient name wrap onto 2-3 lines at 1024px, where there wasn't 150px of spare room to begin with. It now ramps from 0px right at the 1001px handoff from the mobile stack up to the full 150px by ~1255px (via a CSS `clamp()` keyed to viewport width), matching how much room actually opens up as the viewport widens -- confirmed via Playwright that 1024px now wraps exactly as it did before this change (no new wrapping), while 1200px/1440px/1920px all show the full 150px shift with the fan reading as a clean, wide, symmetrical spread.
  - Verified with Playwright at 1024/1200/1440/1920px: the tube's measured vertical center matches the ingredient list's measured vertical midpoint within a few pixels at every width (previously off by 200-300px, sitting well below center); all 16 gold lines still render and connect to the correct ingredient; no new horizontal overflow at any width (the pre-existing 320px marquee-track quirk, unrelated to this change and present in every prior version, is still the only one).
  - Delivered as `HoniOra_V3_LiteDesktop_v48.zip`.

Prior changes (v47):
- New section, "10 / Pomma+ & Urolithin A", added after the Livaux section (renumbering Lemon Water Upgrade 10->11, Taste 11->12, Format 12->13) and linked from the hex nav and the burger-drop-left popup menu, alongside the existing Livaux link. Researched from real, verifiable sources (PubMed/DOI, checked individually rather than taken from a single secondhand summary) rather than written from assumed knowledge, given the topic's genuine nuance. Framed honestly, matching this site's existing citation conventions: Pomma+ supplies punicalagins and ellagitannins, the precursor compounds; Urolithin A is what the gut's own bacteria convert those into, not something Pomma+ itself contains or is standardised for. The section covers: what Urolithin A is and its mitophagy mechanism (Ryu et al. 2016, Nature Medicine, PMID 27400265); the real human clinical record for muscle strength/endurance, which used purified, directly-dosed Urolithin A, not pomegranate extract (Liu et al. 2022, JAMA Network Open, PMID 35050355; Singh et al. 2022, Cell Reports Medicine, PMID 35584623); "urolithin metabotypes", the documented, person-to-person variation in how much Urolithin A anyone actually produces from the same ellagitannin intake (Tomás-Barberán et al. 2017, PMID 27158799; González-Sarrías et al. 2017, PMID 27879044); an explicit statement that nobody, including Stauber, has tested Pomma+ itself for Urolithin A production, so no dose or guaranteed-conversion claim is made; and a plain mechanistic-complement note against Cr-01 Creatine (different systems -- fast ATP regeneration vs. longer-term mitochondrial quality control) that explicitly says no published trial has tested the two together, so no synergy claim is made either.
- Deliberately left out of the new section, on the same honest-citation principle as the rest of the site: a specific percentage of people who are Urolithin A "non-producers" (metabotype research doesn't converge on one clean, citable figure for this) and any claim that Pomma+ and creatine have been shown to work better together (no such trial exists in the literature as of this research).
- New active -- Pomma+ Pomegranate Extract -- ported in from the vNNN line at 500 mg per serving (250 mg/tab), positioned immediately after Livaux everywhere it appears: the hero marquee ticker, the hero-actives curated list (now 16, was 15), the ishot ingredient photo gallery (added `pomma_pomegranate.jpg`/`-lite.jpg`, copied over from the vNNN line's own assets), the full stack-matrix bill-of-materials table (now 29 rows, was 28, with the same honestly-framed mechanism paragraph that doesn't attach a dose-response claim Stauber doesn't itself make), "The Protocol" phase timeline (04 / Year round), the Heart triad quadrant's ingredient list, a new AskHoni chatbot KB entry plus starter chip, the AskHoni "what is HONIORA" summary answer (28->29 actives, 10,949->11,449 mg), the meta description, the hero stat card, the Pack section stats (5,474.50->5,724.50 mg per tablet, 10,949.00->11,449.00 mg per serving), the "16 named actives" hero stat and the "29 named actives, zero blends" stat counter, the Single Pack plan-list ingredient count, and the footer trademark disclaimer list.
- Not changed, matching a gap already present in the vNNN line this was ported from rather than introducing a new one: the Heart triad quadrant list doesn't include Hytolive either (a pre-existing gap on the vNNN line, flagged there but not fixed); the footer's "Third-party marks" trademark line also doesn't list Hytolive or Pomma+, matching vNNN's own text exactly; and the "5.4745 grams of full dose potent actives" line in "The Unit" section still shows the pre-Pomma+ per-tablet total (should be 5.7245 g) -- also unchanged in the vNNN line itself, so left matching rather than fixed here first.
- Marquee text and order, ported from the vNNN line (v406-408): the first ribbon message now leads with "WELCOME TO THE FUTURE OF HEALTHINESS :"; the second ribbon message changed from "Introducing HONI*ORA* to add some sparkle to your day &gt;&gt;&gt;" to "Introducing Honi*Ora* to your day will add to Your Healthiness"; and the ticker's ingredient order changed so MGO 850+ Mānuka Honey leads, immediately before Cr-01 Creatine and Balanced Hydration Matrix (previously Cr-01 led, with MGO third). The ticker's seamless-loop clone is generated dynamically in JS from this same markup, so this one edit covers both copies.
- Verified with Playwright: the hero-actives list, ishot gallery, stack-matrix table and Heart quadrant list all show Pomma+ in the right position with the right dose; the "29"/"16"/"11,449 mg" stat counters read correctly; the marquee's HTML now matches the vNNN line's `.hrail-set` block exactly (diffed byte-for-byte). Full fifteen-width overflow sweep -- zero new overflow, the same pre-existing 320px marquee-track quirk as every prior version.
- Burger/lite-nav icons: checked against the vNNN line on request and found already current -- same hexagon SVG buttons, same burger icon, same popup menus, with this branch's own extra sizing rules for showing the nav at desktop widths too (which vNNN doesn't do). No change made; confirmed with the user directly rather than guessing at an edit.
