Prior changes (v46):
- Headline/copy port from the vNNN line (v403): eyebrow, `<h1>`, tag, precede and precede-lead/solution font sizes all bumped to match vNNN's current sizing; the old "HUGE pills..." `.precede-huge` span removed; headline copy changed to "UPGRADE / YOUR / WELLBEING" with a new "Welcome to the future of healthiness" line beneath the eyebrow. This branch's own `#precedeFit`-driven fit-to-width script (different function names than vNNN's -- `fitHeroLines()`/`matchHeroTagWidth()`/`matchLinesToTagWidth()`/`capPrecedeSolution()`/`fitManukaCaption()`, called from this branch's own `fitHeroAll()`) was kept and still drives this text, rather than porting vNNN's own script wholesale.
- Hero layout restructure, on request, to bring this branch in line with the vNNN line's stacked layout (video/copy banner, then the marquee, then tube+list) rather than its original always-side-by-side two-column hero. This branch's hero video is a portrait, glass-on-white studio product shot (606x904) rather than vNNN's landscape lifestyle video, so rather than copying vNNN's literal full-bleed edge-to-edge banner with an absolutely-positioned overlay copy card (which would make a portrait video absurdly tall), the video and headline copy are grouped into a new, plainly-contained `.hero-banner` row -- centered, not edge-to-edge -- keeping this branch's own video-then-copy pairing while still giving the marquee and the tube+list row their own separate rows below it, matching vNNN's structure.
- The marquee (`.hrail`, plus the wax-seal medallion beside it) moved from sitting before `.hero-lower` entirely to sitting inside `.hero-lower`, between the new `.hero-banner` and the tube column -- given the same oversized-width-plus-negative-margin full-bleed treatment vNNN's v408 uses for its own marquee, keyed to this branch's own single-tier 220px reservation (this branch never had vNNN's two-tier 430px/220px hybrid-range split).
- Added `centerTubeCap()`, ported from vNNN's v408 (same measure-real-position-then-apply-margin approach, wired into this branch's own `fitHeroAll()` cycle) so the tube's cap sits on the hero's true horizontal center rather than wherever the column's leftover space happened to place it.
- Two layout bugs found and fixed during verification, both specific to this branch's new banner row: (1) giving `.hero-banner` the same `width:calc(100% + 220px)` full-bleed formula as the marquee had no effect, because its base rule used `flex:0 0 100%` -- an explicit, non-auto flex-basis, which wins over the `width` property in the flex sizing algorithm (the same width-vs-flex-basis trap the mobile marquee fix hit on the vNNN line) -- changed to `flex:0 0 auto` with an explicit `width:100%` so the override applies at all; (2) once that was fixed, the row still wrapped the video and copy onto separate lines specifically around 1001-1040px, because flex-wrap's line-breaking decision goes by each item's un-shrunk flex-basis, not its final post-shrink size -- the copy's flex-basis was fixed at its full 480px target width, so the browser wrapped rather than shrinking it slightly to fit. Lowered the copy's flex-basis to 320px (with flex-grow:1 and max-width:480px still capping its final size), so the row now stays side-by-side at every desktop width from 1001px up, expanding the copy back to its full width whenever there's room.
- Verified with Playwright across ten widths (1920 down to 320px): the tube cap sits within 0.03px of the hero's true center at every desktop width; the marquee reaches exactly the viewport's left and right edges at every width, including the 1001-1200px range; a full fifteen-width overflow sweep shows zero new horizontal overflow (the marquee's own horizontally-scrolling track exceeding the viewport at 320px is the same pre-existing, by-design quirk already documented in every earlier version). Screenshots confirm a clean side-by-side video+copy banner at 1920/1600/1440/1200/1024px, and the correct video-then-copy-then-marquee-then-tube-then-list stacked order on the mobile layout (991px and below). The burger nav popup was also re-confirmed opening correctly, with no console errors, at both a desktop and a mobile width.
- Delivered as `HoniOra_V3_LiteDesktop_v46.zip`.

Prior changes (v43):
- Interaction: added a one-time scroll-triggered intro to the ikigai four-ring diagram (`#ikigaiDiagram`). The first time it crosses 50% into the viewport, the four rings (Strength, Gut, Heart, Brain) fill their gold colour one at a time in a random order, roughly 300ms apart -- the same visual build as hovering them by hand, just automatic and shuffled so it doesn't play the same way twice. Once all four have landed, the gold core fades in behind "HoniOra" same as it does on a full hover-through, holds for about a second and a half, then the whole diagram clears back to its plain black-on-white outline. From that point on it's the existing hover-to-build behaviour, unchanged. The intro plays once per page load and does not replay on repeat scrolls past the section. Skipped entirely when the visitor's OS has reduced motion turned on -- the diagram just sits in its outline state until hovered, as it always has.
- Implementation: the ring-build and clear logic was factored out of the existing hover handlers into shared `updateCenter()`/`clearAll()` functions so the intro and the hover interaction can't drift out of sync with each other. The intro itself uses an `IntersectionObserver` (threshold 0.5) that unobserves itself after firing once, a Fisher-Yates shuffle of the four ring elements, and a `setTimeout` stagger; no new library, no change to the existing CSS transitions.
- Verified: Playwright confirmed, over repeated runs, that the four rings land in a different order each time, that the gold core only activates once all four are seen (never earlier), that everything clears back to zero `seen` classes automatically, and that ordinary hover-to-build still works correctly on the diagram after the intro has finished (hovering a single ring adds only that ring's colour and the white centre label, `mouseleave` clears it). Screenshot after the intro clears confirms clean black outline on white, matching the original resting state exactly. Full twelve-width overflow sweep (1920px down to 320px) shows zero new overflow -- the same two pre-existing, already-confirmed-unrelated quirks at 561px/28px and 320px/35px are still the only ones present, unchanged from v42.
- Delivered as `HoniOra_V3_LiteDesktop.zip` (replaces the v42 package at the same filename).

Prior changes (v41):
- Fix, nav dropdown swap: `.burger-drop-left` (the popup belonging to the LEFT burger, `#burgerLeft`, which controls the Protocol-themed menu) was anchored with `right:0`, and `.burger-drop-right` (the RIGHT burger's popup, the Founding/2-Tabs menu) was anchored with `left:0` -- the exact opposite of their own burgers' `left:0`/`right:0` anchors. That crossed anchoring is what opened the left burger's menu on the right side of the screen and the right burger's menu on the left. Swapped them so each popup now anchors to the same edge as its own burger (`.burger-drop-left{left:0}`, `.burger-drop-right{right:0}`) and opens directly beneath it. The 1001px+ "near-logo" breakpoint, which repositions both burgers and both popups together, was already internally consistent and untouched.
- Fix, popups too wide on phones: `.burger-drop` had a flat `width:230px` at every width. On phones (both popups can be pinned open at once), two 230px popups left almost no gap between them -- on a ~390px-wide screen they very nearly met edge-to-edge in the middle, reading as one solid block rather than two menus. Changed to `width:min(230px,45vw)`, so the popup still measures a full 230px from ~511px upward (tablets, the lite-desktop range, and ordinary desktop monitors are visually unchanged) but scales down proportionally on narrower phones, leaving a clear gap between the two panels.
- Verified: with both burgers clicked open together, measured each popup's live left/right edges at 390/430/561/768/1000px -- the left popup (Protocol content) now sits flush against the left burger and the right popup (2 Tabs/Founding content) sits flush against the right burger at every one of those widths, with a visible gap between them on phones. Screenshots at 390px and 1000px confirm this visually. Full width sweep across all twelve widths -- zero new overflow, same two pre-existing quirks as prior versions (561px/320px, unrelated to the nav).

Prior changes (v35):
- Fix, hero copy overflowing its box: the two JS shrink-to-fit functions behind the HONIORA wordmark, the tag line, "2 fizzy tablets...", and "Born in New Zealand > Made in USA" all measured `container.clientWidth` as "available space" without subtracting the container's own left/right padding -- fine before v33/v34 added real padding to `.hero-copy-shift`, but once that padding existed it overstated the usable width and let text render past the box's real right edge (worst around 1440px, where "ORA" hung ~61px outside the box). Added a small `innerWidthOf()` helper that subtracts the measured container's own padding, and used it everywhere these functions treat `clientWidth` as available space.
- Fix, same overflow in the narrower 1001-1400px "hybrid" range: at these widths the reserved copy column is already narrow, and neither "HoniOra's Solution:" nor "2 fizzy tablets..." had ever been JS-managed there at all (one had no id to hook into; the other's safety clamp only ran above 1400px) -- so both could overflow the box with nothing checking. `matchLinesToTagWidth()`'s safety clamp now always runs, at every width, not just above 1400px, and a new small `capPrecedeSolution()` applies the same shrink-to-fit safety net to "HoniOra's Solution:" specifically.
- Fix, huge gap between the tube shot and the ingredient list: `.hero-actives` (the ingredient list) sits in normal document flow directly below the headline block, so its old `margin-top: calc(56.25vw + 32px)` -- copied from the tube shot's own rule -- stacked a full video-height on top of wherever the headline's own (variable) height already put it, instead of landing 32px below the video like the tube does. That opened a ~700-800px dead gap between the tube and the list at wide desktop widths. Replaced with a small JS function (`positionActives()`, in the hero-keylines script) that measures the headline's real bottom edge and the video's real bottom edge live, and sets the exact margin needed to clear whichever is lower by a fixed ~56px gap -- correct at every breakpoint, no hardcoded offset standing in for the headline's height. Also nudged the tube shot itself another 40px further below the video, for visual balance against the now much closer list.
- Verified: a full-box overflow check (querying every descendant of `.hero-copy-shift` for a right edge past the box's own) came back clean (zero overflowing elements) at every width from 1001px to 1920px, including the specific 1200-1400px band that had a second, previously-uncaught overflow on "HoniOra's Solution:". Confirmed via direct measurement that the tube shot and ingredient list now start close together (e.g. at 1920px, the list's first item now begins before the tube shot even ends, rather than ~600px below it) at 1024/1440/1920px, and confirmed by screenshot that the gold connector-line fan (which measures both endpoints live, so needed no separate code change) draws correctly to the repositioned list. Confirmed the mobile/tablet hero (<=1000px) is pixel-unaffected -- the ingredient list keeps its own plain flow margin, and the hero still uses the original glass video. Full width sweep across all twelve widths -- zero new overflow beyond the same two pre-existing, already-confirmed-unrelated quirks, no new console errors.

Prior changes (v33):
- Layout, back to the original side-by-side hero: reverted the desktop hero's structure to the original two-column layout (the base, non-media-query CSS the whole site was already built on) -- image column on the left, copy column on the right, matching the site's original design and the user-supplied reference screenshot of it. This replaces v31/v32's full-width, stacked-block treatment.
- Feature, video as a true full-screen background: the video (still the same `<video>` element, still sourced from `hero-gold-loop.mp4`/`.webm`) is pulled out of the left column's normal flow with `position:absolute` and re-anchored to `.hero-lower` (the full two-column row) instead, so it spans the complete row width behind both columns rather than being confined to the narrower left column. Sized to its own natural 1280x720 aspect ratio (width:100%, height:auto) -- nothing is cropped, and the rendered height (about 563px at 1001px wide, 1080px at 1920px wide) reads as a "first screen" backdrop. A negative z-index and a transparent `.hero-lower`/`.hero-img-col` background put it behind the row's own in-flow content (tube shot, copy column) with no extra z-index needed on those.
- Fix, hidden containing-block trap: the left column's existing `transform:translateX(...)` (from the base rule, used to nudge the image column over) was silently establishing its own containing block for the video's `position:absolute`, confining the "full row width" video back down to just that narrow column's box despite `position:static` being set on it. Reset to `transform:none` in this block so the video correctly resolves against `.hero-lower` instead.
- Fix, tube shot floating over the video: `hero-pack-tube.png` is a flat, opaque product photo with no alpha channel -- on the original plain white background its own white backdrop was invisible, but floating directly over the new colourful video turned it into a visible white rectangle sitting on top of the image. Rather than editing the source asset, the tube shot's top margin is now calculated (`calc(56.25vw + 32px)`, matching the video's own 9:16-of-full-width natural height) so it lands just below the video's bottom edge, back on the plain page background -- the same relationship it always had in the original layout, just measured against the new background video's height instead of the old contained image's.
- Fix, right-column spacing restored: `.hero-lower`'s `padding-right` (dropped to 0 in v31/v32 for the full-bleed treatment) is restored to `max(var(--pad),220px)`, the value the two-column layout needs to give the copy column its reserved room.
- Layout, ingredient list and connector-line "fan" also moved below the video: the ingredient list (`.hero-actives`) got the same treatment as the tube shot -- a `calc(56.25vw + 32px)` top margin landing it just below the video's bottom edge, so its lower rows aren't sitting over the video either. The gold connector-line "fan" (the hero-keylines script) needed no separate fix at all: it's a JS-drawn SVG that measures the tube's and each list item's real on-screen position live, so once both endpoints cleared the video the lines between them cleared it automatically.
- Fix, a second hidden cascade trap: the ingredient list's margin-top override was initially silently overwritten by the pre-existing `.keyact` rule further down the file (same single-class specificity, later in source, using a shorthand `margin` that resets all four sides). Fixed by upgrading the selector to `.hero-lower .hero-actives`, which outranks it regardless of source order -- the same category of bug as the `.hero-glass-img` max-height issue caught in v32.
- Feature, translucent box behind the hero copy: added a `rgba(255,255,255,.5)` background and padding to `.hero-copy-shift` specifically (the headline/tag/eyebrow block that still sits over the video by design) so that text stays legible against the video's busy, colour-varying background. Deliberately scoped to just that inner block, not the outer `.hero-grid` column wrapper -- that wrapper turns out to contain the ingredient list, stats and CTA buttons too, all of which are now well below the video where a white box would just look like a stray rectangle on the plain page background.
- Verified: Playwright confirmed the video renders at full `.hero-lower` row width (not just the left column's width) at every breakpoint tested (1920/1440/1024/1001px), at its exact natural aspect ratio with zero cropping, and still playing/looping. Confirmed the tube shot and the ingredient list's first item both land below the video's bottom edge at every width, with no visible white-box artifact and no text crossed by the video. Confirmed the translucent copy box sits correctly behind just the headline block. Confirmed the mobile/tablet hero (<=1000px) is pixel-for-pixel unchanged via screenshot. No anchor-scroll code changes were needed.

Prior changes (v31):

The `media="(max-width:1000px)"` sources are untouched, so the mobile/tablet hero keeps the original glass-fizzing video exactly as before -- confirmed by the user's follow-up "revert to previous video on lite" request, which was already the plan before that message arrived.

- Fix, cascade conflict: a pre-existing `@media(min-width:1001px)` rule further down the file set `.hero-lower{padding-right:max(var(--pad),220px)}`, which -- being later in source order -- would have silently stopped the video from reaching the true right edge. Changed to `padding-right:0` so "full screen width" is actually full width.

Rather than hiding the tube shot (which would have sent every connector line to the page corner), it's kept visible and repositioned into the video band's bottom-left corner.
- Feature, hero copy width-matching (desktop only, >1400px, same scope as the existing `matchHeroTagWidth`): a new `matchLinesToTagWidth()` function measures each line's natural rendered width and scales its font-size so "2 fizzy tablets & 1 glass of water & 60 sec." and "Born in New Zealand > Made in USA" render at the same width as "MĀNUKA CREATINE PROTOCOL" above them, using the same measure-then-scale technique already used elsewhere on the page. A safety clamp prevents either line from ever overflowing its column if a future copy change makes an exact width-match impossible.
- Verified: Playwright confirmed the video element is present, playing, looping, muted, and full-bleed (945px tall at a 1050px-tall/1440-wide viewport, i.e. 90vh, 100% width), with the four `<source>` tags split correctly (only the two unconditional ones point at the new clip). Confirmed the breakpoint switch is clean at the 1000/1001px boundary -- 1000px still shows the original static layout and video, 1001px shows the new full-width video treatment. Confirmed the width-matching lines land within a fraction of a pixel of the target width at 1440px and 1920px (previously off by roughly 2% due to a sub-pixel rounding issue in the safety clamp, now fixed). Full width sweep re-run across 12 breakpoints -- zero new overflow beyond the same two pre-existing, already-confirmed-unrelated quirks (561px, 320px), no new console errors.

Prior changes (v29):
- Layout, panel translucency: `.burger-drop` background changed from `rgba(176,130,31,.95)` to `rgba(176,130,31,.9)`, per the explicit `alpha = 0.90` given in the request.
- Feature, both popups stay open together: previously clicking a burger closed the other one first (mutual exclusion). The click handler now toggles each popup fully independently, so a visitor can pin both open at once and compare both lists before choosing.
- Feature, choice always closes both: clicking any link, in either popup, still closes both popups (not just the one the link was in) -- unchanged from v28's behaviour, now re-verified now that both can be open together.
- Fix, hover-to-choice dead zone: found and fixed a real bug while verifying the "rollover and pick a link with no click" requirement. `.burger` sits vertically centred in the 66px navbar while `.burger-drop`'s `top:100%` was measured from the navbar's own bottom edge, leaving a ~14.5px gap between the burger's actual bottom edge and the popup's top edge. A mouse moving down off the burger toward the popup crossed that gap, lost `:hover`, and -- since `pointer-events` reverts to `none` the instant `.burger:hover` stops matching -- could never re-acquire hover once it reached the popup, so the popup just faded out from under the cursor. Fixed by pulling `.burger-drop`'s `top` up by that same amount inside the existing `@media(hover:hover)` block only, so the fix applies to mouse users exactly where it matters without touching the click/tap-pinned behaviour or the differently-sized navbar at phone widths.
- Verified: Playwright confirmed both popups can be pinned open simultaneously by clicking each burger in turn, that clicking the same burger again unpins just that one (the other stays open), and that clicking a link in either popup closes both and navigates to the right anchor. Confirmed the panel background computes to exactly `rgba(176, 130, 31, 0.9)`. Confirmed, with a realistic straight-down-then-across mouse path, that the popup stays visible continuously from hovering the burger through moving into the popup through hovering a link inside it, and that mouse-clicking that link (no burger click at all) closes the popup and navigates correctly. Full width sweep re-run -- zero new overflow beyond the same two pre-existing, already-confirmed-unrelated quirks, no new console errors. Screenshot confirms both popups pinned open at once at 1440px.

The `#askHoniBtnMobile` link that lived inside `#mmRight` is gone with it; its JS wiring line is removed too.
- Feature, burger popup promoted to the real menu: `.burger-drop-left`/`.burger-drop-right` (the v27 hover-only preview) is now the site's sole nav menu at every width, including phones. It still shows on hover for mouse users, and now ALSO pins open on click/tap via a new `.burger-drop.open` state (defined outside the `hover:hover` gate so it works on touch devices too) -- once pinned it stays open regardless of mouse position until a link inside is clicked, the other burger is clicked, or the visitor clicks anywhere else on the page, any of which closes it. Since it's the only menu now rather than a decorative duplicate, `aria-hidden="true"` and every link's `tabindex="-1"` were removed, and each burger's `aria-controls` now points at its popup's own id (`burgerDropLeft`/`burgerDropRight`) with `aria-haspopup="true"` added. Added `max-height`/`overflow-y:auto` to the popup so all 9 links stay reachable on short viewports now that it's load-bearing at phone widths.

Prior changes (v8):
- Media, hero glass video replaced: the user's supplied clip (43.8s, 822x462 HEVC, portrait via rotation metadata) is a static-camera macro shot of a HONIORA tablet fizzing in a glass tumbler on a countertop, with a blurred window/wood background visible in two vertical strips either side of the glass and around its base. Since the camera and glass never move for the whole clip (confirmed by comparing background-only regions between frames 43 seconds apart: near-zero pixel difference), a single background-removal pass was enough: ran the frame through an ML background remover (u2netp) at two widely-separated timestamps, averaged the two resulting alpha mattes for a clean, noise-reduced silhouette, and reused that one static matte across every frame of the selected loop -- no per-frame segmentation needed.
- Media, loop selection and seam: picked a 4.0s window (source 7.0-11.0s) with good bubble activity and a bright, on-brand golden colour that hadn't yet darkened too far. Composited each frame's matched matte onto solid white (matching the site's `--paper` background) so it blends into the hero exactly like the video it replaces. Because the drink's colour keeps deepening throughout the source clip, a straight cut loop would jump visibly at the seam -- fixed by crossfading the last 0.6s of the loop into its own opening 0.6s (`xfade`), so playback wraps with no visible jump.
- Media, file size: replaced `hero-glass.mp4`, `hero-glass.webm`, `hero-glass-lite.mp4`, `hero-glass-lite.webm` and `hero-glass-poster.jpg` in place (same filenames, no HTML changes needed). New combined size across all five files is about 1.7MB, versus about 4.3MB for the files they replaced -- smaller despite being new content, per the request to keep file size down. Canvas is 462x822 (down from 606x904); lite variants scaled to 336x598.
- Verified: Playwright confirmed the hero video element reports `readyState:4`, the correct new pixel dimensions (462x822 desktop, 336x598 mobile-lite), and no load/decode errors, at both a desktop width (1440px) and a mobile width (390px). Full width sweep re-run -- zero new horizontal overflow beyond the same two pre-existing, already-confirmed-unrelated quirks (28px at 561px, 35px at 320px). Screenshot at 1440px confirms the glass renders cleanly against the white hero background with no leftover window/countertop, and the crossfaded loop seam was checked frame-by-frame (last frame vs first frame) and is visually indistinguishable.

Prior changes (v7):
- Layout, panel translucency: `.mm-col` background changed from `rgba(255,255,255,.3)` to `rgba(255,255,255,.4)`.
- Verified: Playwright confirmed both `.mm-col` panels compute to `rgba(255,255,255,0.4)`. No other rules touched.

Prior changes (v6):
- Colour, nav-list links: `.mobilemenu a` previously had no explicit colour (it inherited the page's near-black `var(--ink)`, #15170F), and two links ("Join Founding List", "Ask Honi") carried an inline `style="color:var(--gold)"` making them gold instead. Set every nav-list link to solid `#000` by default via `.mobilemenu a{color:#000}`, added a new `--brown:#6B4423` custom property, and added `.mobilemenu a:hover,.mobilemenu a:focus-visible{color:var(--brown)}` for the rollover state. Removed the two inline gold overrides so all sixteen links -- across both panels -- now behave identically: black at rest, brown on hover/focus.
- Layout, panel translucency: `.mm-col`'s background changed from `rgba(255,255,255,.5)` (50% translucent, set in v3) to `rgba(255,255,255,.3)` (30% translucent), so more of the hero content behind the open panels shows through.
- Verified: Playwright confirmed all 16 `.mobilemenu a` links compute to `rgb(0,0,0)` at rest and `rgb(107,68,35)` (`#6B4423`) on hover, and both `.mm-col` panels compute to `rgba(255,255,255,0.3)`. Full width sweep re-run -- zero new horizontal overflow beyond the same two pre-existing, already-confirmed-unrelated quirks (28px at 561px, 35px at 320px). Screenshot with both panels open at 1440px confirms black link text throughout (no leftover gold), brown-on-hover, and the increased see-through against the hero glass behind. No new console/page errors beyond the site's known pre-existing sandbox noise.

Prior changes (v5):
- Layout, exact overhang split: `.hexbtn-m img,.hexbtn-m svg` height changed from the vw-scaled `clamp(56px,6vw,80px)` to a fixed `71px` (66px navbar height + 5px). A `clamp()` was dropped here specifically because holding an exact 0px/5px split at every desktop width needs a size that doesn't drift with viewport width the way a vw-scaled term does.
- Layout, top-aligned instead of centred: `.hexbtn-m.nav-hex-mobile-left` and `-right` switched from vertical centring (`top:50%`/`translateY(-50%)`) to `top:0`, so the hex's top edge now sits flush with the navbar's top edge and all of the extra height (5px) falls below the bar instead of splitting evenly above and below.
- Verified: Playwright measured `getBoundingClientRect()` overhang and gap values at four widths (1001, 1200, 1440, 1920px). Every width returned exactly `overhangTop:0, overhangBottom:5` on both hexes, with a consistent ~10px clear gap to the neighbouring burger -- no overlap at any tested width. Full width sweep re-run separately: zero new horizontal overflow beyond the same two pre-existing, already-confirmed-unrelated quirks (28px at 561px, 35px at 320px). A cropped screenshot at 1440px visually confirms the hex's top edge flush with the header top and its bottom edge dipping slightly past the navbar's bottom border, with the burger icon sitting clear to its side.

Prior changes (v4):
- Layout, hex overhang: `.hexbtn-m img,.hexbtn-m svg` max height raised from `clamp(48px,5vw,68px)` to `clamp(56px,6vw,80px)`. The hexes are vertically centred on the 66px-tall navbar (`top:50%`/`translateY(-50%)`), so at 80px tall they now clear the bar by 7px top and bottom -- a deliberate, visible overhang rather than the near-flush fit of v3.
- Interaction, both panels close together: previously, clicking a nav-list link only closed the panel it was in (`wire()`'s per-link handler called `col.classList.remove('open')` on just that column). If a visitor had opened both the left and right sliding panels independently, clicking a link in one left the other sitting open. Replaced that with a shared `closeBoth()` that clears both `.mm-col`s and both burgers' `aria-expanded` on any link click. When only one panel was open, this behaves exactly as before -- clearing an already-closed panel is a no-op.
- Verified: Playwright re-ran the full width sweep -- zero new overflow, same two pre-existing quirks. Bounding-box check confirmed the hexes now overhang the navbar by exactly 7px top and bottom with no overlap against the burgers (10px clear gap). Opened both panels, clicked a link inside the right one, and confirmed both `#mmLeft` and `#mmRight` lost their `open` class and the body scroll lock released. No new console/page errors beyond the site's known pre-existing sandbox noise.

Prior changes (v3):
- Layout, edge placement: at 1001px+, `.burger`/`.burger-left` and the two `.hexbtn-m.nav-hex-mobile-*` reserve hexes previously sat inset by `.wrap`'s own side padding (`var(--pad)`, up to 64px), same as normal page content. Added an override (placed after the unconditional nav rules so it wins the cascade) that pulls both out toward the true browser-window edge: the burgers sit about 4px off the literal edge, with the hexes just inside them. The mini reserve hexes were also sized down further, from `clamp(64px,7vw,92px)` to `clamp(48px,5vw,68px)`.
- Layout, nav-list panels: the left sliding panel's links (`#mmLeft`) were left-aligned within their own half of the screen, which put that list hugging the outer left edge, while the right panel's links (`#mmRight`) -- also left-aligned, but within a box that starts at the centre divider -- already read as hugging the centre. Added `text-align:right` to `.mm-col:first-child` so the left list's text also reads toward the centre divider, mirroring the right one. Each link's own box (and its divider line) still spans the full column width -- only the text shifts, not the row.
- Layout, panel background: `.mm-col`'s background changed from the opaque `var(--paper)` (#FFFFFF) to `rgba(255,255,255,.5)`, a 50%-translucent white, so the hero content behind the open panels shows through.
- Verified: Playwright re-ran the full width sweep (1920 down to 320px) -- zero new horizontal overflow, same two pre-existing quirks as v1/v2. Bounding-box check confirmed no overlap between the burger and its neighbouring reserve hex at 1440px (10px clear gap). Screenshot with both panels open at 1440px confirms the translucent background (hero glass and headline visible through both), the left list's items right-aligned against the centre divider matching the right list, and the hexes/burgers sitting near the true screen edges with no clipping. No new console/page errors beyond the site's known pre-existing sandbox noise.

Prior changes (v2):
- Layout, size correction: `.hexbtn-m img,.hexbtn-m svg` and `.brand-mobile .brand-logo` carried the same size rules from 561px all the way up through this branch's new unconditional desktop range. That was fine on a real tablet-width screen, but at true desktop widths (1440px and up) the rest of the page -- hero, headline, wordmark-adjacent content -- renders at full desktop scale while these two elements stayed capped at their tablet-era maximum (hexes topping out at 160px, logo flat at 58px), reading oversized against the user's own reference template. Added a `@media(min-width:1001px)` override that scales both down for this range specifically: hex icons to `clamp(64px,7vw,92px)` (was `clamp(108px,19.5vw,160px)`) and the wordmark to 46px (was 58px). The narrower 561-1000px tablet band, already shipped and already matching the user's first reference screenshot, is untouched -- it keeps the original sizing.
- Layout, matching clearance: `.mm-col`'s top padding (added so the reserve hexes don't hang down over the first nav-list link) was sized for the old 160px-max hexes. With the hexes now topping out at 92px in this same 1001px+ range, reduced the matching clearance to 130px (was 190px) so the panels stay just as tight without leaving dead space above "Protocol" / "2 Tabs".
- Verified: Playwright re-ran the full width sweep (1920 down to 320px) -- zero new horizontal overflow, same two pre-existing quirks as v1 (28px at 561px, 35px at 320px), nav still computes to `display:grid`/burger `display:block` everywhere. Screenshots at 1440px (top of page, left menu open) confirm the reserve hexes now sit fully within the header band without overlapping the ingredient marquee ticker beneath it, matching the user's reference screenshots. 390px mobile screenshot confirms no change below 1001px. No new console/page errors beyond the site's known pre-existing sandbox noise.

Prior changes (v1):
- Layout, nav default: the nav's switch-over from the full text-link hex nav to the "lite" burger + mini-hex + sliding-panel nav previously happened at `max-width:1400px` (`.navbar{display:grid;...}`, hiding `.hexnav-item`/`.nav-cluster`/`.brand-center`/`.nav-cta`/`.hexnav-logo`, showing `.burger` and the two `.hexbtn-m.nav-hex-mobile-*` reserve hexes). That upper bound is removed, so this lite nav is now unconditional -- the default at every viewport width, including ordinary desktop monitors that never triggered it before. The full hex-text nav's own CSS rules are untouched and still in the file, just permanently overridden to `display:none` now rather than only below 1400px.
- Layout, matching rules brought along: three companion rules that shared the same 1400/1401px thresholds as the nav switch (and exist specifically to support the lite nav's look) lost their upper bounds too, for consistency: the `.lite-br` line-break (previously hidden above 1401px, now always shown), the hero headline's "HUGE pills...Every morning?" line (previously hidden only below 1400px, now always hidden, matching the lite headline treatment), and the eased `.hero-lower` padding-right / `.hero-precede .precede-fit` font-size tuning (previously 1001-1400px only, now 1001px and up). None of these apply below 1000px, where the hero was already stacking into a single column regardless of nav mode -- untouched.
- Layout, list-panel top clearance: `.mm-col`'s `padding-top:190px` override (added originally so the tall 160px-max reserve hexes don't hang down over the first link in each panel) was capped at `max-width:1400px`; since the same tall hexes now render at every desktop width too, that cap is removed so the same clearance applies everywhere the lite nav does.
- Verified: Playwright confirmed `.navbar` computes to `display:grid` and `.burger` to `display:block` at every width tested (1920, 1440, 1200, 1024, 900, 768, 600, 561, 560, 390, 375, 320px) and that the full hex-text `.hexnav-item` nav computes to `display:none` throughout. Zero horizontal overflow at every width except two pre-existing quirks unrelated to this change (28px at 561px, 35px at 320px -- both already present before this branch and confirmed unrelated in earlier versions of the vNNN line). Screenshots at 1440px (top of page, left menu open, right menu open) and 390px (top of page, left menu open) compared directly against the user's own reference screenshots of the site at a ~1000-1400px viewport: nav bar layout, reserve-hex placement, headline copy, and both nav-list panels (item order, "Join Founding List" and "Ask Honi" gold highlight colour, divider lines, top clearance before the first item) all match. No new console/page errors beyond the site's known pre-existing sandbox noise.
- Design note: this branch only changes which nav presentation is the default and the handful of rules tied to that same breakpoint. It does not change the hero's two-column desktop layout (image left, copy right), which was already independent of nav mode and continues to switch to a stacked single column at `max-width:1000px`, same as before.

Base content inherited from the vNNN line (v400, then v399):
- Layout: `.cr01-logo` (the purple Jenerise Cr.01 badge image, `jenerise-cr01-logo.png`, in the `05 / Creatine Evolved` section) had `border-radius:14px`, giving it visibly rounded corners against the section's cream background. Changed to `border-radius:0`, so the badge renders as a hard-edged square, matching the source artwork's own square shape.
- Interaction: the ikigai-style four-ring diagram's hover script (`updateCenter()`) previously only added the `seen` class to the centre "HoniOra" label -- which switches its fill to white via the existing `.ikigai-center.seen{fill:#fff}` rule -- once every one of the four rings (Strength, Gut, Heart, Brain) had been hovered in turn. Changed it to also track `anySeen` (true the moment a single ring gets its `seen` class on `mouseenter`), and the centre label's `seen` toggle now follows `anySeen` rather than `allSeen`. The centre core shape's full gold-background reveal (`#ikigaiCore.seen`) still waits for all four rings, unchanged -- only the label's white-text switch was moved earlier. Visually this reads correctly because even a single ring's colour fill already extends under the centre label (the four circles are positioned to overlap there), so white text is legible from the very first rollover, not just once all four have built up. `mouseleave` on the whole diagram still resets every ring and the centre label/core back to their default state, unchanged.
- Verified: Playwright confirmed the centre label carries no `seen` class before any hover, gains it after hovering just one ring (with the ring's own `seen` count at 1 and the core still not `seen`), and loses it again on `mouseleave` from the diagram. Screenshot with only the Strength ring hovered confirms "HoniOra" renders in clean white against that ring's green fill. Zero horizontal overflow at 1440px and 390px, no new console/page errors beyond the site's known pre-existing sandbox noise.

Prior changes (v398):
- Layout: wrapped two spans of the `05 / Creatine Evolved` section's second lede in `.nowrap-line` spans -- "Our creatine is sourced from Jenerise, a new company by creatine's own UK pioneers, Steve and Rachael Jennings." and "Steve first developed and introduced creatine to the British Olympic Team in 1992. Jenerise Cr-01 Creatine Monohydrate" (the rest of that sentence, "is evolutionary, built to...", continues wrapping normally after it). Added `@media(min-width:1200px){#problem .sec-head .lede .nowrap-line{white-space:nowrap}}`, so each of these spans renders on a single line from 1200px up.
- Design constraint, flagged rather than silently worked around: these are long sentences (roughly 850-1120px wide at this section's type size), and this paragraph's container tops out at 1132px even at the widest desktop viewport. Below approximately 1180px-1200px, forcing either sentence onto one line pushes the page into horizontal scroll -- measured overflow ranging from ~28px at 1150px up to ~590px at 320px if forced unconditionally. There's no way to fit either full sentence on one line below that width without either horizontal scroll or shrinking the text well past this section's established minimum size. The nowrap treatment is therefore scoped to 1200px and up; below that, both sentences wrap normally across 2-4 lines depending on viewport, same as before this change, with zero overflow preserved at every standard breakpoint (1440px, 1024px, 900px, 768px, 600px, 480px, 390px, 375px).
- Verified: Playwright across eleven widths from 320px to 1440px. Zero horizontal overflow at every one except a pre-existing 35px overflow at 320px that was already present in v397 before this change (confirmed by testing the unmodified v397 package at the same width) and is unrelated to this edit. Screenshot at 1440px confirms both sentences render as single lines. No new console/page errors beyond the site's known pre-existing sandbox noise.

Prior changes (v397):
- Layout: added a non-breaking space between "Rachael" and "Jennings." in the `05 / Creatine Evolved` section's second lede, so that pairing can never split across a line at any viewport width (matching the existing "Creatine Monohydrate" nowrap treatment from v392).
- Copy: "Steve developed and introduced creatine monohydrate first to the British Olympic Team in 1992." changed to "Steve first developed and introduced creatine to the British Olympic Team in 1992." -- moves "first" earlier in the sentence and drops "monohydrate" from this mention, so Steve is credited with developing and introducing creatine generally rather than creatine monohydrate specifically. The following sentence ("Jenerise Cr-01 Creatine Monohydrate is evolutionary...") is unchanged, including its existing non-breaking space on "Creatine Monohydrate."
- Verified: Playwright at 1440px, 768px, 390px, and 375px. Zero horizontal overflow at any width. Confirmed via DOM query the paragraph carries the nbsp on "Rachael Jennings." and reads exactly as specified above at every width. Screenshot at 375px confirms "Rachael Jennings." stays on one line. No new console/page errors beyond the site's known pre-existing sandbox noise.

No wording changed, only the added break. This also resolves the tablet-width 5-line wrap flagged in v395 -- splitting the paragraph at this point means it no longer needs to rely on the container's full available width to stay compact.
- Verified: Playwright at 1440px, 768px, and 390px. Zero horizontal overflow at all three. Confirmed via DOM query the paragraph's HTML includes the `<br>` at exactly this point at every width, with no other wording changed. Prior changes (v395):
- Copy: the `05 / Creatine Evolved` section's second lede changed from "Our creatine is sourced from creatine's own UK pioneers, Steve and Rachael Jennings...." to "Our creatine is sourced from Jenerise, a new company by creatine's own UK pioneers, Steve and Rachael Jennings...." -- introduces Jenerise by name as the company, ahead of naming its founders. Added commas around "a new company by..." for readability; no other wording in the request was changed. Rest of the paragraph (Steve's 1992 introduction, the "is evolutionary" claim, the SGS verification) untouched. The non-breaking space on "Creatine Monohydrate" (from v392) stays in place.
- Known trade-off, flagged rather than silently fixed: this addition makes the paragraph noticeably longer, and the tablet-width line-wrap fix from v394 (`min(90vw,1132px)`, tuned to keep this paragraph at 4 lines around 768px) no longer holds with the extra text -- at 768px it now wraps to 5 lines again. The paragraph's container is already at its full available width at that breakpoint (no room left to widen further without overflowing the page), and its font size is already at the design's floor (17px, the bottom of the `clamp(17px,1.85vw,21px)` scale), so closing the gap again would mean either shortening the copy or overriding the type scale below its established minimum for this one paragraph -- neither done here without asking first.
- Verified: Playwright at 1440px (4 lines), 768px (5 lines, see trade-off above), and 390px. Zero horizontal overflow at all three. Confirmed via DOM query the paragraph reads exactly as specified above. No new console/page errors beyond the site's known pre-existing sandbox noise.

Prior changes (v394):
- Layout: `#problem .sec-head .lede`'s max-width formula changed from `min(83.2vw,1132px)` to `min(90vw,1132px)`. This paragraph's width was previously capped tighter than the available space in roughly the 640px-900px viewport range (tablet widths, e.g. iPad portrait at 768px), which forced the second lede paragraph to wrap across 5 lines instead of 4. Widening it to use the space actually available inside `.wrap`'s padding brings 768px (and the 768px-900px range generally) down to a clean 4-line wrap, matching how it already read at wider desktop widths. No copy changed. Desktop (1440px, unaffected: still wraps to its existing line count) and mobile (560px and below, governed by its own separate `max-width:none` rule) are untouched.
- Verified: Playwright measured the paragraph's actual wrapped line count (via `Range.getClientRects()`, not just visual inspection) across nine widths from 320px to 1440px. Confirmed 768px now wraps to exactly 4 lines with zero horizontal overflow, and re-confirmed zero overflow at the standard 1440px and 390px checks. Screenshot at 768px confirms a clean 4-line paragraph. Note: a narrower band around roughly 660px-740px still wraps to 5 lines and 561px-640px to 6, since the paragraph is already using the full width available inside the page's side padding at those widths and widening further would overflow the layout -- flagged here in case that narrower range also needs attention.

Prior changes (v387):
- Interaction: every hex nav item's dropdown preview (`.hexdrop`) opens on `:hover`/`:focus-within`. Clicking a nav link already navigated to the correct anchor (a separate JS smooth-scroll handler owns that and was already correct for every item), but the dropdown itself stayed visibly open afterward, for two separate reasons: (1) a clicked link keeps browser focus, so `:focus-within` stays true; (2) the header is `position:fixed`, so the just-clicked button stays under the visitor's actual mouse cursor even after the page scrolls away, and a real cursor doesn't move on its own just because the page did, so `:hover` also stays true. Fixed both: a new click handler blurs the trigger immediately (clears the focus half), and adds a `js-hexdrop-suppress` class that force-hides the dropdown via `!important` regardless of hover state (clears the hover half), removing that class again the instant the mouse actually leaves the trigger so the very next normal hover still opens it. Applies uniformly to every hex nav item, including the Ask Honi button (which opens a modal instead of navigating) -- its dropdown now closes the same way.
- Verified: Playwright at 1440px, clicking each of Function, Stack, GLP-1, Join Honi, and Livaux and confirming (a) the URL hash lands on the correct target for every one, (b) the dropdown is fully hidden (`opacity:0`/`visibility:hidden`) immediately after the click while the mouse is still resting on the same spot, and (c) moving the mouse away and re-hovering afterward still opens the dropdown normally, confirming the fix doesn't disable hover going forward. Same check on Ask Honi: modal opens, its dropdown closes immediately, and hover still works normally after the modal is closed. Re-ran the full standard check at 1440px desktop and 390px mobile: zero horizontal overflow at both, no new console/page errors beyond the site's known pre-existing sandbox noise.

Prior changes (v386):
- Layout: the hex nav's default dropdown-card rule centres every card under its own button (`left:50%` + `transform:translateX(-50%)`), except the first item (`Reserve Honiora`) and last item (`Join Honi`), which already had a dedicated pulled-inward rule so their 210px-wide card can't run off the edge of the screen. GLP-1 sits immediately to the left of Join Honi -- one item away from that same tight right edge -- so its centred card still extended right far enough to sit half under Join Honi's own button, reading as if the two dropdowns belonged to each other (confirmed against 5 reference screenshots comparing GLP-1's dropdown against the correctly-templated Function, Stack, and Join Honi ones). Gave GLP-1's `<a>` a new `hexbtn-nextlast` class carrying the same right-anchored treatment as `hexbtn-last` (`left:auto;right:0`, no centring transform, arrow re-centred on the trigger's own middle via `.hexdrop-card::before{right:18px}`), so its card now sits fully under itself with no overlap into Join Honi's space. Function, Stack, and every other mid-row item are untouched -- they were already positioned correctly and served as the template for what "correct" looks like.
- Verified: Playwright at 1401px, 1420px, 1440px, and 1600px (spanning the narrow end of the desktop nav down to the point flagged in the existing code comments as the worst case for edge overflow). Measured every dropdown's bounding box against its own trigger button at each width: GLP-1's card right edge now matches its button's right edge exactly (previously it extended ~170px further right, into Join Honi's own column), with zero horizontal page overflow and no clipping at any width tested. Screenshots at 1440px confirm GLP-1's dropdown now reads the same way Function's and Stack's already did -- sitting cleanly under its own trigger -- and that Join Honi's own dropdown is unaffected. Re-ran the full standard check at 1440px desktop and 390px mobile: zero horizontal overflow at both, no new console/page errors beyond the site's known pre-existing sandbox noise (blocked CDN tunnel, missing favicon 404).

Prior changes (v385):
- Layout: `.askhoni-thread`'s `min-height` doubled from `160px` to `320px`, so the Ask Honi modal opens noticeably taller before any conversation fills it in. On its own this would have exceeded the modal's old height ceiling on shorter viewports (especially mobile, where the 86vh cap plus 5vh top/bottom padding left too little room, clipping the input box and footer off the bottom), so two supporting changes went with it: `.askhoni-modal`'s vertical padding changed from `5vh` to a fixed `12px` (horizontal `4vw` padding unchanged), and `.askhoni-panel`'s `max-height` changed from `86vh` to `calc(100vh - 24px)`. Together these reclaim the screen space the doubled thread needs so the full panel, including the input field and footer disclaimer, stays on-screen at both breakpoints.
- Verified: Playwright at 1440px and 390px, opening the modal through its real trigger (`#askHoniBtn`'s click handler, `ahOpen()`) rather than a simulated class toggle, so the actual welcome message and starter chips render as a real user would see them. Zero horizontal overflow at either breakpoint. Confirmed via bounding-box math that the panel's full content, header through footer, sits inside the viewport at both breakpoints (mobile: panel content 809px against 820px of available height, footer bottom at 826.5px against an 844px viewport). Screenshots at both viewports confirm no clipping and a visibly taller modal. No new console/page errors beyond the site's known pre-existing sandbox noise.

Prior changes (v383):
- Copy: `#glp1`'s lede changed from "Not a synthetic analogue, not one ingredient hoping to do everything. Three separate, independently dosed actives working four separate angles on your GLP-1, the hormone that manages your appetite and blood sugar." to "Not a synthetic peptide analogue, not one ingredient hoping to do everything. Three, independently and fully dosed natural actives working four support mechanisms for your own GLP-1, the hormone that manages your appetite and blood sugar." Four word-level changes: "synthetic analogue" -> "synthetic peptide analogue" (more precise -- both native GLP-1 and the drugs that mimic it are peptides); "separate, independently dosed actives" -> "independently and fully dosed natural actives" (keeps the independently-dosed claim, which matches the dossier's per-ingredient dose verification, adds "fully dosed" and "natural"); "four separate angles on your GLP-1" -> "four support mechanisms for your own GLP-1" ("mechanisms" matches the named mechanisms in the dossier -- FFAR2, cAMP, propionate fermentation, eriocitrin -- more precisely than "angles"; "support...for" is the standard supplement-copy framing, safer than "angles on" which reads closer to a direct-action claim). The explicit `<br>` line break after "everything." (added in v380) is preserved.

Prior changes (v374):
- New section: added `#glp1`, a three-card section ("Three routes. One goal: your own GLP-1.") right after the Function section and before Clinical Thresholds. Each card covers one of HONIORA's three GLP-1-related actives, independently fact-checked before writing (see the project's GLP-1 dossier doc): (i) MGO 850+ Mānuka/Fiberest® HF -- credits the GLP-1 mechanism to the Fiberest HF fibre's fermentation into short-chain fatty acids acting on the FFAR2 receptor, not to the honey, since no independent evidence ties Mānuka honey itself to GLP-1; cites Tolhurst 2012 (Diabetes, the FFAR2 mechanism paper) and a 2026 Frontiers scoping review of 52 human trials that found dextrin-type fibres (Fiberest HF's own class) the strongest performer for GLP-1 of any fibre type tested. (ii) Feiolix® Feijoa -- the two-route framing (abscisic acid on gut L-cells; a separate fibre fermented by gut bacteria into propionate), cited to Guri 2015 (PLOS ONE, the actual L-cell/cAMP mechanism paper, cell-line plus rat) alongside the existing supplier "Ingredient science" link. (iii) Eriomin® Lemon Flavonoid -- unchanged from the already-verified claim (three real human RCTs at the on-site 200 mg/day dose), reusing the exact three citations already on the Stack table row. A closing line notes these are published trials on the individual ingredients or mechanism-level lab/animal research, not a trial of the finished HONIORA product, matching the disclaimer style already used in the Clinical Thresholds section. Built with the existing `.pillars`/`.pillar` card grid (already used in the Function section) and the existing `.cite`/`.cite sup` citation-link styles (already used throughout the Stack table), so no new CSS was needed.
- Nav: replaced the `RITUAL` hex nav item with a `GLP-1` item linking to `#glp1`, in the same slot between Ask Honi and Join Honi, keeping the same hexdrop dropdown-card pattern. The `#ritual` section itself (01 / The Ritual) is untouched and still reachable from the mobile menu's "Ritual" link and its own in-page anchor; it's just no longer in the primary hex nav, per the request to replace that specific button.
- Copy: added a GLP-1 mention to the hero's MGO 850+ Mānuka / Fiberest® HF ingredient caption -- now ends "...feeds your beneficial gut barrier microbes, whose fermentation of that fibre also supports your own natural GLP-1," matching the fibre-not-honey framing used in the new GLP-1 section.
- Verified: Playwright at 1440px and 390px. Zero horizontal overflow at either breakpoint. Confirmed via DOM query that `RITUAL` no longer appears in the hex nav and `GLP-1` does, linking to `#glp1`; confirmed `#glp1` renders with all three pillar cards and 7 citation links total; confirmed `#ritual` still exists in the page (untouched, just unlinked from the hex nav); confirmed the MGO caption's `<i>` text now contains "GLP-1". No new console/page errors beyond the site's known pre-existing sandbox noise. Screenshots of the new section at both breakpoints confirm clean single-column stacking on mobile and a clean 3-column card grid on desktop, and a screenshot of the header nav bar confirms GLP-1 renders correctly in the RITUAL slot with no clipping or overlap.

Prior changes (v373):
- Interaction: `.hero-seal-inner`'s hover/focus transform changed from `scale(2)` to `translate(-40px,40px) scale(2)` -- on rollover (or keyboard focus) the badge now moves 40px left and 40px down at the same time as it doubles in size, instead of growing in place. `translate` is listed before `scale` in the transform value specifically so it composes in the final, already-scaled coordinate system (CSS transform functions apply right-to-left in the element's local space) -- this gives an exact, scale-independent 40px shift rather than the shift itself getting doubled by the 2x growth. Same existing `.3s ease` transition on `.hero-seal-inner`, so the move-and-grow animates smoothly together. No change to the badge's resting position/size, the ring's spin, or the v371 hover z-index bump to 150 (still needed -- the badge now reaches even further toward the fixed header on hover than the in-place double did).
- Verified: Playwright's real `.hover()` (not a simulated class toggle) at 1440px and 390px. Computed `transform` on `.hero-seal-inner` while hovered resolves to `matrix(2,0,0,2,-40,40)` at both breakpoints, confirming the translate component is the raw -40/40 requested, not doubled. Bounding-box centre deltas measured before/after hover matched exactly: desktop moved from (1304, 218.234375) to (1264, 258.234375), mobile from (325, 150.15625) to (285, 190.15625) -- both exactly (-40, +40). Zero horizontal overflow at either breakpoint before and after triggering hover, no new console/page errors beyond the site's known pre-existing sandbox noise. Screenshots at both viewports confirm the enlarged, shifted badge renders fully visible with no clipping against the header.

Prior changes (v371):
- Interaction: `.hero-seal`'s hover/focus scale on `.hero-seal-inner` changed from `scale(1.07)` to `scale(2)` -- the badge (bee wax-seal photo plus the rotating Romanesque-lettering ring around it) now visibly doubles in size when rolled over or keyboard-focused, using the same existing `.3s ease` transition so it grows smoothly rather than snapping. Also bumped `.hero-seal`'s own `z-index` to 150 on hover/focus (from its resting 40) -- the badge sits just below the fixed header near the top of the page, and growing to 2x from its own centre reaches up into the header's screen area at most scroll positions, so without this the enlarged badge would render partly hidden behind the header (z-index 100). No change to the badge's resting size, position, rotation, or the ring's spin animation/wording.

Prior changes (v369, reverted in v370 -- kept here for the record):
- Layout: applied the same `-50px` horizontal nudge already on the hero copy block (from v365) to two more elements the user identified as part of "this block": `.hero-tube-shot` (the gold tube image, in the image column) and the first `<li>` of the actives list, "MGO 850+ Mānuka Honey crystals" (the rest of that list stays put). Prior changes (v366):
- Removed: the HoniBlog "click-to-embed YouTube Shorts, play inline in place" script (added v352), including the `referrerpolicy`/`allow` attributes added to its iframe in v358. The `.blogplay` buttons no longer intercept the click -- they're plain `<a href="https://youtube.com/shorts/<id>" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">` links again, opening YouTube in a new tab, exactly as they were before v352. Confirmed the user's GitHub Pages hosting platform does not support custom HTTP response headers at all (official GitHub statement, GitHub community discussion #54257: "We don't support this feature today so a `meta` tag unfortunately is the only way" -- and that "only way" still can't override the host's own header if one is stricter than desired, per MDN's documented precedence), which meant this site had no page-level lever left to try for the inline-embed approach. Removing the iframe removes the error along with it, and also removed the now-unused document-level `<meta name="referrer">` tag added in v358 (it existed only to support the removed inline embed).
- Verified: zero horizontal overflow at 1440px and 390px, no new console/page errors. Simulated a click on `.blogplay` and confirmed the event is no longer prevented and no iframe gets created (`defaultPrevented:false`, `iframeExists:false`), and confirmed the link's `href`/`target`/`rel` are unchanged from their original external-link values.

- Investigated (no fix shipped that version): the user reported error 153 was still occurring on the HoniBlog YouTube embeds despite the v358 fix. Researched further and found the v358 code comment's core assumption -- that a page-level meta tag overrides the host's own `Referrer-Policy` HTTP header -- is very likely wrong per MDN and a closely-matching real diagnosed case (Simon Willison's blog, GitHub issue simonw/simonwillisonblog#561). See v366 above for the resolution.
- Verified: zero horizontal overflow at 1440px and 390px, no new console/page errors beyond the site's pre-existing intermittent missing-favicon noise and blocked-CDN noise.

Prior changes (v361):
- Fix: the Romanesque text ring was silently truncating -- "LADIES AND GENTLEMEN...." showed, but "UPGRADE...." never rendered. Cause: the ring's `<textPath>` started at `startOffset="25%"` along the circle but then asked for `textLength="804"` (the full circumference) starting from that point, which runs 25% past the path's own end -- an SVG path has a fixed length, it doesn't loop, so the renderer simply stops drawing once it runs off the end, chopping off the last quarter of the string. Fixed by redrawing the circle `<path>` to start exactly at the top (12 o'clock) instead of the left (9 o'clock), and setting `startOffset="0"` -- text now starts where the path starts, uses the full 804-unit length without overrunning it, and reads exactly the same top-to-clockwise layout as before with nothing missing.
- Changed: ring text simplified from "LADIES AND GENTLEMEN.... START YOUR UPGRADE...." to "LADIES AND GENTLEMEN START YOUR UPGRADE", per the user's exact wording that version (no ellipses). Added trailing spaces in the source text to keep a visible gap at the seam where the ring loops back from "UPGRADE" to "LADIES" -- discovered along the way that SVG collapses/trims whitespace by default same as HTML, so `xml:space="preserve"` had to be added to the `<text>` element for those trailing spaces to actually render as a gap instead of disappearing.
- Restored: the ring's slow continuous rotation (removed in v360 at the user's request, then asked back) -- `animation:heroSealSpin 34s linear infinite` on `.hero-seal-ring`, with the `@keyframes` and a `prefers-reduced-motion` override restored alongside it. The bee photo at the centre stayed fixed at its 20deg rotation, unaffected -- only the text ring spun (clockwise, later reversed in v362).
- Verified: headless-browser check confirmed `textPath.textContent` included the complete phrase, a full-page (not element-cropped) screenshot showed "LADIES AND GENTLEMEN" and "START YOUR UPGRADE" both fully legible with a clear gap at the seam, and the ring's computed transform matrix changed between two points in time (confirming the rotation was actually running, not just declared). Zero horizontal overflow at 1440px and 390px, no new console/page errors beyond the site's pre-existing missing-favicon 404.

Prior changes (v360):
- Layout: moved the `.hero-seal` medallion from dead centre of the hero to the top-right corner, just below the actives ribbon (`.hrail`). Moved its markup to live inside `.hrail` itself (which is already `position:relative`) and switched positioning from `top:50vh;left:50%;transform:translate(-50%,-50%)` to `top:calc(100% + 14px);right:var(--pad)` -- flush under the ribbon's own bottom edge and against the same right padding every other edge-aligned element uses, regardless of the ribbon's own responsive height. No longer overlaps the "UPGRADE YOUR RITUAL" hero copy at any width. Shrank the badge slightly (clamp(112px,13vw,192px) -> clamp(96px,10vw,150px) desktop, clamp(108px,28vw,148px) -> clamp(78px,20vw,110px) phone) to suit a corner badge rather than a dead-centre one.
- New: rotated the bee wax-seal photo 20deg clockwise (`transform:translate(-50%,-50%) rotate(20deg)` on `.hero-seal-img` -- the translate keeps it centred, the rotate turns the image itself around that same centred point).
- Removed: the medallion's continuous spin animation (restored in v361 at the user's request). Deleted `animation:heroSealSpin...` from `.hero-seal-ring` and the then-unused `@keyframes heroSealSpin` / `prefers-reduced-motion` override -- the Romanesque text ring was static for this one version. The hover/focus scale-up on the whole badge was untouched.
- Verified: headless-browser check at 1440px confirmed the medallion sat just under the ribbon, flush right, with zero overlap with any hero copy; clicking it set `location.hash` to `#store` and scrolled there like RESERVE HONIORA. Confirmed clean placement at 390px too. Zero horizontal overflow at either width, no new console/page errors beyond the site's pre-existing missing-favicon 404. (What wasn't caught at the time: the text ring was actually truncating before "UPGRADE" -- fixed in v361.)

Prior changes (v359):
- New: added a circular wax-seal medallion to the dead centre of the hero (screen one) -- the user's supplied photo of a gold bee wax seal, cut out (rembg, u2netp model) to a clean transparent circle, with a Romanesque-caps ring of text (`Cinzel`, added from Google Fonts alongside the site's existing Inter/Playfair Display) reading "LADIES AND GENTLEMEN.... START YOUR UPGRADE...." around its full circumference, set on an SVG circular text path sized to exactly wrap the ring (`textLength`, `lengthAdjust="spacingAndGlyphs"`). The medallion spun slowly and continuously (later removed in v360), scaled up slightly on hover/focus, and sat centred via `top:50vh` (not 50%) so it landed in the middle of the first viewport specifically, regardless of how much taller the hero section grows with its own content below the fold. Wrapped in a link to `#store` -- the same target as the RESERVE HONIORA nav item -- so it picks up the site's existing smooth-scroll click handling automatically. New files: `bee-seal-badge.png` / `-lite.png` (760px / 420px, transparent PNG, pngquant-optimised). Caught and fixed one bug along the way: the badge's inner wrapper was a bare `<span>` with `width:100%;height:100%` in its CSS -- spans are inline by default and inline elements ignore explicit sizing, so the badge was invisible until `display:block` was added to that wrapper.
- Layout: `#problem` ("05 / Creatine") now lands right under the fixed nav bar when reached via any of its nav links (the top hex nav's CR.01 CREATINE, and the mobile menu's Cr.01 Creatine), instead of the large empty gap every other section link leaves. Every `.sec` on the site carries clamp(80px,11vw,152px) (64px flat on phones) of top padding before any visible content, and the site's custom in-page scroll JS (`scrollToHash`) normally lands each section right at that padding's start -- consistent everywhere except that the user specifically wanted this one section tighter, matching a reference screenshot. Read the section's own live padding-top via `getComputedStyle` (so it tracks the current viewport's clamp()/breakpoint value automatically, no hardcoded per-breakpoint guesswork) and net it out of the existing header-clearance offset, scoped to `#problem` only -- every other section's landing position is untouched. Also added a matching `#problem{scroll-margin-top:...}` CSS override (again computed against the section's own padding) for the one path the JS doesn't cover: a shared link landing directly on `#problem` on first page load at desktop widths, which uses the browser's native anchor-scroll rather than a click.
- New: renamed the section's eyebrow label from "05 / The Creatine Dose" to "05 / Creatine Evolved".
- Hardened fix: v358's YouTube error 153 fix (an iframe-level `referrerpolicy` attribute) can still be defeated if the hosting platform sends its own stricter `Referrer-Policy` HTTP response header (common on some static hosts/CDNs) -- some browsers apply that document-level policy ahead of the per-iframe one. Added `<meta name="referrer" content="strict-origin-when-cross-origin">` to the page's `<head>`, which takes precedence over the host's own header in every major browser, as a second, more robust layer under the existing iframe attribute (kept as-is).
- Verified: headless-browser check at 1440px confirms clicking CR.01 CREATINE in the nav now lands with the "05 / Creatine Evolved" eyebrow about 18px below the nav bar (matching the user's reference screenshot), the Jenerise link and portrait sizing from v358 are unaffected, and the new meta tag is present with the iframe's own `referrerpolicy` attribute still intact. Confirmed the hero medallion rendered correctly at 1440px and 390px, clicking it set `location.hash` to `#store` and scrolled there exactly like RESERVE HONIORA does, and it did not introduce any horizontal overflow at either width. Also found, while testing, that on this sandbox's phone-width emulation, in-page anchor links (this one and others, e.g. STACK) land noticeably short of their target section -- confirmed this is a pre-existing site behaviour unrelated to that version's changes (reproduces identically on an untouched link), not something introduced there.

Prior changes (v358):
- Fix: the "Ingredient science Jenerise" link under the 3,000 MG Cr-01 Creatine Monohydrate stat (`#problem`, "05 / The Creatine Dose") now points to `https://www.jenerise.com/creatine-101` instead of the site root `https://www.jenerise.com/`. Confirmed via search that this is the only `<a href="https://www.jenerise.com/...">` link on the page (a second, unrelated plain-text mention of "jenerise.com" lives inside the AskHoni chatbot's knowledge-base source field, not a link, and was left alone).
- Layout: reduced `.jj-people figure` width about 10% (`clamp(230px,29vw,336px)` -> `clamp(206px,26vw,302px)`, desktop max 336px -> 302px, mobile min 230px -> 206px) and tightened the gap between the two portraits by more than half (`clamp(40px,8vw,64px)` -> `clamp(22px,4.5vw,36px)`, desktop max 64px -> 36px) so Rachael sits noticeably closer under Steve, per the user's request for both portraits slightly smaller and Rachael lifted closer to Steve.
- Fix: the HoniBlog click-to-play YouTube embeds (added in v352) were throwing YouTube's player error 153 ("video player configuration error") when clicked. Researched the cause: this error is commonly triggered when an embedded YouTube iframe doesn't send the referrer information the player needs to initialise (some browsers/hosting setups omit or strip it by default). The documented fix is to set an explicit `referrerpolicy` on the iframe; added `referrerpolicy="strict-origin-when-cross-origin"` to the iframe the click handler creates. No change to the embed domain (still `youtube-nocookie.com`) or to the video ids.
- Verified: headless-browser check at 1440px and 390px confirms the Jenerise link's new href, both portraits render at the new smaller size with the new tighter gap (302px/36px desktop, 206px/22px mobile, confirmed via `getBoundingClientRect()`), and clicking a HoniBlog play button now creates an iframe carrying `referrerpolicy="strict-origin-when-cross-origin"` alongside the correct video id. Zero horizontal overflow at either width, no new console/page errors beyond the site's pre-existing missing-favicon 404. Actual YouTube playback inside this sandbox couldn't be confirmed end-to-end (no outbound access to youtube-nocookie.com from this environment) -- worth checking on the live, deployed site that error 153 is actually gone; if it persists there, the next thing to check is whether the hosting platform or a CDN/proxy in front of it is itself stripping the Referer header on outbound requests, which would need a host-side fix rather than a page-side one.

Prior changes (v357):
- Fix: removed the white ring that showed around the outside of the purple circle on both portraits. Cause: the v354 images were composited as a 720px purple disc centered on an 820px white square (matching this site's ingredient-photo "colour disc on white" convention), but the `<img>` itself is clipped to a circle by CSS (`border-radius:50%`) at the box's own full width -- so the CSS circle (radius = half the box) was larger than the purple disc inside the image, and the gap between them showed as a thin white ring. Fix was at the image level, not CSS: regenerated both portraits from the same rembg cutouts (no change to the cutouts themselves) as full-bleed purple squares -- the purple now fills the entire 820&times;820 (and 480&times;480 `-lite`) canvas edge-to-edge, so the CSS circle clip shows solid purple all the way to its edge, no ring. Same files, same filenames (`steve_jennings_portrait.jpg` / `-lite.jpg`, `rachael_jennings_portrait.jpg` / `-lite.jpg`), replaced in place.
- Layout: increased `.jj-people figure` width 20% (`clamp(192px,24vw,280px)` -> `clamp(230px,29vw,336px)`) -- desktop max 280px -> 336px, mobile min 192px -> 230px. Gap between the two portraits left unchanged, per the user's request (bigger portraits only, not asked to change spacing).
- Verified: headless-browser check at 1440px and 390px confirms both circles now show solid purple to the very edge (no white ring visible), both render at the new larger size (336px desktop, 230px mobile, confirmed via `getBoundingClientRect()`), the stack is still centered and Rachael still sits directly below Steve. Zero horizontal overflow at either width, no new console/page errors beyond the site's pre-existing missing-favicon 404.

Prior changes (v353):
- New: added item 05 to the `.prob-list` "What it does" list in `#problem` ("05 / The Creatine Dose"): "Electrolytes support creatine uptake," summarising three mechanisms the user supplied -- sodium-driven transport into muscle cells, electrolyte-regulated intracellular fluid balance, and membrane potential/muscle-contraction support -- condensed into the list's existing bold-lead-plus-description format (matching items 01-04) rather than kept as nested sub-bullets, since no `.prob-list` item on the page uses a bullet-within-bullet structure. Tied the claim to HONIORA's own formula rather than leaving it generic: closes by naming the site's existing "Balanced Hydration Matrix" (sodium bicarbonate + potassium bicarbonate + magnesium malate, 1,400 mg/serving, already established on site since v267) as the source of this electrolyte support alongside the Cr-01 Creatine dose.
- Accuracy check: the user's pasted text named "Sodium-glucose linked transporters (SGLT)" as the mechanism pulling creatine into cells. SGLT transporters are glucose transporters, not the creatine-uptake pathway -- creatine crosses into muscle via a separate, dedicated sodium- and chloride-dependent creatine transporter. The site copy states the sodium-dependence (which is accurate and is the substantive point) without naming SGLT specifically, avoiding a wrong technical term while keeping the claim itself intact. The cellular-hydration and muscle-membrane-potential mechanisms were general, uncontested physiology and were summarised as supplied, matching this section's existing style of stating basic mechanism explanations without an inline citation (e.g. item 02's phosphocreatine/ATP explanation carries none either).
- Verified: headless-browser check confirms the list now has 5 items in order, item 05 renders the correct lead and body text, and the section reads cleanly at both 1440px and 390px. No horizontal overflow at either width, no new console/page errors.

Prior changes (v350):
- Fix: the Quadraxial caption's shared `.lede{max-width:62ch}` cap was narrower than its 86-character first half (before the deliberate `<br>`), so that half was wrapping early and pushing the whole caption to 3 lines on desktop instead of the intended 2. Added a targeted `#triad .sec-head p.lede` rule that widens the cap to fit both halves (86 and 78 characters) on their own single line at &ge;1001px, reverting to the original 62ch cap below that (where the section already stacks and a further wrap reads fine). No wording or `<br>` position changed -- only how the existing text now wraps.
- New utility, applied once: added a `.oneline-fit` marker class, scoped as `#problem .sec-head .lede.oneline-fit` (matching/beating the section's existing `max-width:min(83.2vw,1132px)` rule on specificity), that lets a short one-sentence `.lede` render on a single line whenever its section is wide enough, falling back to a normal wrap the moment it isn't -- rather than `white-space:nowrap`, which would either overflow or (given this site's `overflow-x:clip` on `body`) silently clip text on narrower screens. Applied it to "Creatine is one of the most trusted and heavily researched ingredients in wellness today, used well beyond athletics." under 05 / The Creatine Dose, which now holds to 1 line at &ge;1280px (matching the site's own 1260px content-width token, with margin to spare) and wraps normally below that -- 2 lines from about 1000&ndash;1279px, 3&ndash;4 lines on phones. The second, much longer "Sourced from creatine's own UK pioneer..." paragraph right below it is untouched: same class (`.lede`), no `.oneline-fit` marker, so its own wrapping measure is unaffected.

Previous version (v349): In "05 / The Creatine Dose" (the pinned two-track scroll section, `#tablet`), swapped the stacking order so the ingredient photo scroll now sits above the feature-claim word scroll.

Recent changes in this version:
- Layout: moved the `.iband` block (the ingredient photo scroll, e.g. Cr-01 Creatine, M&#257;nuka crystals) above the `.unit-vp` block (the word/bullet scroll, e.g. "HONIORA tablets each contain 5.4745 grams...", "Gluten Free") within their shared `.unit-stage` flex container. Both tracks stack in plain document order (`.unit-stage{display:flex;flex-direction:column}` on desktop and in the `.unit.static` mobile fallback alike), so this was a pure markup move: no CSS or JS changed, and neither track's own horizontal scroll behaviour, colours, or content changed at all.
