Confirmed on mobile (390px, the `.unit.static` stacked fallback that real visitors at &le;1000px get) that the ingredient photo grid now renders above the word/bullet list, exactly as requested. This sandbox has no network path to the GSAP/ScrollTrigger CDN (a known, previously documented limitation, not new to this version), so the true desktop pinned-scroll mechanic could not be exercised live here; confirmed instead by code review that `.unit-stage` uses the same document-order flex stacking in both the pinned-desktop and static-fallback states, with no `order` property or JS logic anywhere keyed to which track comes first, so the same reordering applies identically to the real desktop experience.

Previous version (v348): Repositioned the ikigai diagram's four rings in "04 / The Strength, Gut, Heart, Brain Matrix" -- Brain now sits at the top, Heart at the left, Strength at the bottom (Gut stays at the right, unchanged) -- and trimmed a trailing line break from the Quadraxial formula caption beneath it.

Changes in v348:
- Layout: the four rings and their labels swapped slots so Brain occupies the top position, Heart the left, and Strength the bottom; Gut was not mentioned and stays exactly where it was, at the right. Each ring's colour and hover behaviour is keyed to its pillar identity (via its existing CSS class and `data-pillar`/`data-pillar-label` attributes), not its position, so this was a pure markup move -- no colours, no JS logic, and no styling rules changed. Brain keeps its teal `#087282`, Heart its crimson `#B21736`, Strength its olive `#728529`, Gut its orange `#F3701E`.
- Content: removed the trailing `<br>` at the end of the "Quadraxial formula matrix..." caption, per the user's newly re-quoted exact text, which keeps the mid-sentence break after "maximised gut absorption," but no longer adds one after "brain support."
- Verified: zero horizontal overflow and zero console/page errors at 360/390/414/1425/1440px. Read back each ring's `cx`/`cy` and each label's `x`/`y`/text via the DOM, confirming Brain/Heart/Strength/Gut sit exactly at the top/left/bottom/right slot coordinates respectively. Hovered each ring at a point clear of the diagram's overlap zone (label position, not the ring's raw centre, since the four tightly-packed circles overlap heavily near the middle) and confirmed each one lights up in its own correct colour, that hovering all four cumulatively builds the full-colour diagram with the gold centre, and that moving off the diagram resets it. Confirmed clean rendering and correct new layout on mobile (390px, no hover).

Previous version (v347): Made the ikigai diagram in "04 / The Strength, Gut, Heart, Brain Matrix" interactive: each ring builds up its own colour on hover, cumulatively, until all four are coloured and the centre lights up gold.

Changes in v347:
- Interaction: hovering any of the four rings (Strength, Gut, Heart, Brain) fades that ring in from outline-only to its brand colour (Strength olive `#728529`, Gut orange `#F3701E`, Heart crimson `#B21736`, Brain teal `#087282`, matching the user-supplied reference palette). Colour is sticky per ring: moving the mouse from one ring to the next keeps the previously-hovered rings coloured too, so hovering all four in turn builds up to the full-colour version of the diagram rather than resetting with each new hover. Moving the mouse off the whole diagram resets it back to the outline-only state, so the reveal replays on the next visit.
- Colour blending: the four ring fills use `mix-blend-mode:multiply` (with `isolation:isolate` scoping the blend to just the diagram) so pairwise overlaps darken and mix naturally in the browser, the same way overlapping translucent circles behave physically -- no separate overlap colours to hand-author.
- Centre treatment: multiplying four semi-opaque layers on top of each other in the true centre was compounding to near-black, which didn't match the reference's warm gold centre. Added a fifth shape -- the exact four-circle intersection region, computed from the circles' own geometry (radius 130, centres 80px from the middle in each cardinal direction) as an SVG path of four 130px-radius arcs -- filled with the site's own `var(--gold)` and set to appear only once all four rings are hovered, sitting above the multiply-blended rings but below the "HoniOra" label. This keeps the pairwise overlaps naturally blended while guaranteeing the centre reads as intended gold, not mud.

Previous version (v340): Replaced the golden bee's small rocking rotation with a full three-leg 180&deg;/360&deg;/180&deg; sweep, layered on top of its existing wing-buzz jitter, on both bee instances.

Changes in v340:
- Animation: reworked the shared bee rotation. Split rotation out of the `beeBuzz` keyframe (which now only carries the fast sub-pixel `translate()` wing-jitter, via the individual CSS `translate` property) and into a new `beeSwing` keyframe animation (using the individual CSS `rotate` property, so it composes with `beeBuzz`'s translate rather than overwriting it). `beeSwing` runs a fixed three-leg loop, timed proportionally to degrees covered so the angular speed stays constant throughout: 0&deg; to 180&deg; clockwise (first quarter of the cycle), then 180&deg; to -180&deg;, a full 360&deg; sweep anticlockwise (the middle half of the cycle), then -180&deg; back to 0&deg; clockwise (the final quarter), looping continuously at 5.2s per full cycle. Both `#honeytop` and `.closing-bee` run `beeBuzz .1s linear infinite, beeSwing 5.2s linear infinite` together, so the bee vibrates its wings continuously while sweeping through the rotation.
- Compatibility note: this relies on the CSS individual transform properties (`translate`, `rotate`) rather than the older combined `transform` shorthand, specifically so the fast jitter and the slow sweep can run as two independent animations without one overwriting the other's transform value. Supported in all current major browsers (Chrome/Edge 104+, Safari 16.4+, Firefox 72+); the page's existing `prefers-reduced-motion` rule still disables both animations for anyone with that preference set, with no extra code needed.
- Verified: zero horizontal overflow and zero console/page errors at 360/390/414/1425/1440px. Sampled the computed `rotate` property directly on both bee elements across a full 5.2s cycle at 400ms intervals, confirming the value rises to ~180&deg;, continues through 0&deg; down to ~-180&deg;, then rises back toward 180&deg; again -- exactly the requested three-leg sweep, not a shortest-path wrap. Screenshots of both bee instances mid-animation show them clearly rotated off their resting orientation, rendering cleanly with no clipping or layout shift.

Previous version (v339): Added a fast back-and-forth rotational rock to the golden bee's existing wing-buzz jitter, on both bee instances.

Changes in v339:
- Animation: extended the shared `beeBuzz` keyframe animation (already running on both `#honeytop` in M&#257;nuka Honey and `.closing-bee` in the closing section) to add a `rotate()` component alongside the existing sub-pixel `translate()` jitter, at each of the same four keyframe steps: 0deg at rest, +3.2deg, -3.2deg, +2deg. Since both bee instances already share this one keyframe block and the same `.1s linear infinite` timing, the added rock applies to both automatically -- no per-instance changes needed. The rotation alternates sign step to step, reading as a rapid side-to-side rock layered on the existing vibration, rather than a spin in one direction.
- Verified: zero horizontal overflow and zero console/page errors at 360/390/414/1425/1440px. Sampled `getComputedStyle().transform` on both bee elements across five timestamps roughly 40ms apart, confirming the matrix's rotation terms (not just its translate terms) are actively changing sign and magnitude on both instances -- genuine oscillating rotation, not a static tilt. Screenshot at 1440px confirms the closing bee still renders cleanly centered with no clipping from the added motion. All section ids still unique, eyebrow sequence still 01-17.

Covers: the 600 mg per-serving dose; the *Faecalibacterium prausnitzii* result (3.4% to 7.0% of the gut microbiome within 28 days, 8 of 9 participants, from a real, independently-verified randomised controlled trial, PMID 29152256); that Livaux&reg; is a whole-fruit powder rather than one isolated fibre; three numbered facts (Bay of Plenty / Zesy002 (Gold3) sourcing, the 41-compound whole-fruit phenolic matrix vs. plain inulin, and the 25-50 mg per billion CFU probiotic-support figure); and a closing disclaimer distinguishing the cited trial's actual dose (2,400 mg/day, functionally constipated adults) from HONI<i>ORA</i>'s own formulated 600 mg/serving dose, since the two should not be conflated.
- Sourcing discipline: only cited PMID 29152256 because it could be independently corroborated outside PubMed's own CAPTCHA-gated pages (via an aggregator reproducing the actual abstract text/statistics), cross-checked against Livaux's own science page. A second, more recent (600 mg-dose) study referenced elsewhere was deliberately *not* cited, since its exact journal and authorship could not be independently confirmed through any accessible source -- its two manufacturer-claim facts (the Bay of Plenty sourcing and the 41-compound matrix figure) are instead linked directly to livaux.com pages as manufacturer claims, not presented as independently verified research.
- Placement: inserted as "10 / Livaux Gold Kiwifruit" directly after the Stack/Matrix section, requiring 7 eyebrow renumbering edits (10 through 17) rather than the 13 a placement after Mānuka Honey would have needed, while staying narratively coherent (both are single-ingredient deep dives).

Previous version (v313): Two lite-site (mobile/static fallback) requests in "THE UNIT" section: the feature-list dots are now all one size, each line highlights and expands as it's scrolled past (shrinking back as focus moves to the next), and the ingredient icons below now slide in from alternating sides as they scroll into view.

Changes in v313:
- Bug fix: `.upanel.lead::before` was setting the two lead rows' dots to 12px while every other row's dot (`.upanel::before`) was 8px -- exactly the "dots aren't the same size" the user pointed out. Removed the override; every dot in the list is now the plain 8px mark, lead or not.
- New behavior (`.unit.static` only -- the lite/mobile stacked list, and the true GSAP-unavailable/reduced-motion fallback at any width; the desktop pinned-scroll rail is untouched): each `.upanel` row now gets `.active` toggled based on which row's own centre sits closest to the vertical middle of the screen, recomputed on scroll via a `requestAnimationFrame`-throttled listener (gated by an `IntersectionObserver` on the list's own wrapper so it does no work while off-screen) -- the same "closest-to-centre wins" approach the ingredient-icon focus effect already used, just driven by page scroll instead of the pinned GSAP scrub. `.active` scales the row up ~4.5% from its left edge, enlarges its dot, and shifts its color to gold, all via `transition`, so exactly one line is ever highlighted, and it shrinks back the instant focus moves to the next one. Skipped entirely under `prefers-reduced-motion`.
- New behavior (same `.unit.static` scope): each ingredient icon (`.ishot`) now starts hidden and offset (alternating left/right by position) and slides in with a fade once it's ~15% into view, via a second `IntersectionObserver`, revealing once and staying revealed. The hidden/offset starting state is applied by a `.pre-anim` class the script itself adds at boot -- if that script fails or `IntersectionObserver` is unsupported, the icons simply keep the existing always-visible static rule instead of getting stuck invisible. Also skipped under `prefers-reduced-motion`.
- Both new scripts check `unit.classList.contains('static')` before acting and never write to any class the desktop pinned-scroll code reads, so the existing GSAP mechanic (and its own icon `.focus` effect) is unaffected whenever it's actually running.
- Verified: all 9 dots in the panel list report identical computed size (8x8px). Stepped through the list at fine (30px) scroll granularity: `.active` moves through every row in order with never more than one row active at a time, and clears once the whole list has scrolled past (confirmed it does not stay pinned to the last row just because the icon grid further down is still on screen -- an early version of this fix had that bug, caught by watching the list's own wrapper rather than the whole section). Confirmed the active row's computed `transform` and `color` differ from an inactive row's. Confirmed all 16 ingredient icons reach `.in` over the course of a scroll-through, and that `.pre-anim`/`.in` have no visual effect when `.unit.static` isn't present. No overflow at 360/375/390/414/768/1440px. Console-checked at mobile and desktop viewports for JS errors from either new script: none. Full regression re-confirmed: hero centering at 1425px (v303), one-line born/made eyebrow at 360/390/414px (v304), keylines path count matches ingredient count at 15 (v309-v311), Cr.01 logo width doubled and unchanged at 208px/1440px (v310).
- Known test-environment limitation: this sandbox has no network access to the GSAP/ScrollTrigger CDN, so "desktop" checks here exercise the same `.unit.static` fallback path as mobile rather than the real pinned-scroll mechanic. The new code is scoped by construction (checks `isStatic()` before touching anything, and the CSS for both new effects is nested under `.unit.static`) so it stays inert whenever the real GSAP pin is active, but that specific path could not be executed live in this environment.

Previous version (v311): User sent a screenshot showing the gold keylines still not aligning cleanly with the ingredient text -- correctly: v309's fix was position-only, and a visible seam remained where the SVG line met the separate CSS tick.

Changes in v311:
- Bug fix: each gold line was drawn as an SVG path ending just short of a separately-styled CSS element (`.keyact.flat li::before`, a 9px dash marking the last stretch before the text). Even with both perfectly positioned (as of v309), the two pieces met with a visible seam: the SVG line runs at .62 opacity and 1px stroke width, the CSS dash was fully opaque -- so the join read as a small kink/double-mark, zoomed in enough to notice. Fixed by extending each SVG path itself the extra 9px the dash used to cover (`TICK_LEN`), making the entire connector -- curve and tail alike -- one continuous stroke at one consistent weight and opacity, and hiding the now-redundant CSS dash on desktop (`@media(min-width:1001px)`) so it's never drawn twice. Mobile/lite is untouched: it never gets an SVG line, so it keeps the plain CSS dash as its only ingredient marker.
- Verified: confirmed the CSS dash's computed `display` is `none` on desktop (1440px) and still `block` on mobile (390px). Confirmed each path's SVG endpoint now sits exactly 9px past the ingredient text's left edge (matching the removed dash's width precisely). Visual zoom on the join point (6x) shows one clean, continuous line with no seam. Re-checked: item count (15), no overflow at 390/768/1440px, scroll-trigger reveal behavior (v306-v308) untouched, hero centering (v303) and one-line eyebrow (v304).

Previous version (v310): The Jenerise Cr.01 logo in the "05 / The Creatine Dose" section header doubled in size, at the user's request.

Changes in v310:
- Layout: `.cr01-logo` width changed from `clamp(72px,9vw,104px)` to `clamp(144px,18vw,208px)` -- exactly double at every breakpoint (72px to 144px on mobile, 104px to 208px on desktop). Height stays `auto` so it scales proportionally, same as before.
- Checked: rendered logo box measures exactly 144x144px at 390px and 768px widths, 208x208px at 1440px and 1920px widths -- precisely double the v309 measurements at each. No horizontal overflow introduced at 390/768/1440px. Confirmed visually with a screenshot of the section.

Previous version (v309): The gold lines genuinely were misaligned with the ingredient text on a real screen, despite v308's headless check reading as clean -- two real bugs, both fixed.

Changes in v309:
- Bug fix (small, ~1.3px, present since this component was first built): the little gold tick before each ingredient's text (`.keyact.flat li::before{top:.6em}`) was authored slightly high of the text's true vertical center -- measured directly against the actual rendered text (`Range.getBoundingClientRect`), the real center sits at .694em down from the li's top edge, not .6em. Corrected the CSS to `.694em`. The JS that measures where each gold line should end was also switched from reading the tick's own computed CSS position (which v308's alignment check validated against, so it "passed" while checking the wrong reference point) to measuring the ingredient's actual text directly -- removing this whole class of "does the tick's assumed offset match the real text" drift for good, on any browser.
- Bug fix (the bigger one, ~10px, and specific to this reveal): a not-yet-revealed ingredient sits under `transform:translateY(10px)` (part of the fade-up entrance), which shifts its measured position down by 10px. The script had nothing forcing a re-measure the instant an item's own reveal transition finished -- only later, incidental triggers (a resize, fonts loading) or a deferred catch-up rebuild timed to the end of the *whole* staggered reveal -- so every gold line sat a visible 10px low of its own text for the several seconds the reveal was still playing, only snapping into place afterwards. This was the real, visible bug behind the user's report, and it's specific to versions v306 onward: v305's reveal played at page load with no meaningful gap before that catch-up rebuild fired, so the lag was never around long enough to see; once the trigger was pushed later (v306-v308, at the user's request), the lag became the main thing visible during the reveal. Fixed by having the geometry measurement itself account for the pending 10px drop on any item that hasn't reached `.active-in` yet, so every line targets its ingredient's FINAL resting position from the first frame it's visible, not a stale pre-reveal one that self-corrects seconds later.
- Verified: measured every path's real endpoint against a live `Range`-based reading of its ingredient's actual text (not the CSS the code itself uses, an independent ground truth) at multiple points during the reveal -- 300ms, 600ms, 1000ms+ after it starts. Previously: 10px off at 300ms, still 0.6px off at 600ms, correct only by ~1000ms+. Now: correct (0-pixel delta, all 15 items) from the first sampled moment after each item enters `.active-in`. Re-verified the .95 scroll-trigger threshold (v308) is untouched, item count (15), no horizontal overflow at 390/768/1440px, mobile shows the full list with no animation, keylines path count (15), hero centering (v303 fix), and the one-line "Born in New Zealand > Made in USA" eyebrow (v304 fix).

Previous version (v308): Gold-line-to-ingredient alignment checked (incorrectly passing, see above) and the reveal trigger pushed later again, to 95% of the list on screen.

Changes in v308:
- Alignment check (user asked to verify the gold connector lines still land correctly on each ingredient): measured every path's actual SVG endpoint against that ingredient's own real tick-mark position (`.hero-actives li::before`, read live off the DOM, not assumed), across all 15 items including the two-line-wrapping "Balanced Hydration Matrix electrolytes" entry. Every endpoint matched its ingredient's tick position to within half a pixel. Also confirmed visually via a full rendered screenshot at 1440px -- every line lands right at its own ingredient's tick, no drift. No code changed here, just verification.
- Tuning: the ingredient-reveal trigger threshold raised again, from v307's 85% to 95% visible -- essentially the entire list now has to be on screen before the reveal starts. Verified with an incremental-scroll simulation at 1440x900: v307 triggered at ~254px of scroll, v308 triggers at ~311px.
- Re-verified: list stays hidden at load on a normal 1440x900 viewport, still reveals immediately with no scroll on a tall 1440x2200 viewport where the whole hero fits, item count (15), no horizontal overflow at 390/768/1440px, mobile shows the full list with no animation, keylines path count (15), hero centering (v303 fix), and the one-line "Born in New Zealand > Made in USA" eyebrow (v304 fix) -- none of that code was touched.

Previous version (v307): At the user's request, the ingredient reveal added in v306 waited for more of the scroll before it started.

Changes in v307:
- Tuning: the threshold that triggers the ingredient stagger reveal (`.hero-actives` scrolling into view) raised from 50% to 85% visible. On a 1440x900 viewport this pushes the trigger point from roughly 86px of scroll to roughly 254px -- about three times further down the page before the reveal starts. Everything else about v306's fix (the separate, generous reset threshold on the whole hero block, so the reveal doesn't flicker off mid-scroll and still replays cleanly on scroll-up) is unchanged.
- Re-verified: list stays hidden at load on a normal 1440x900 viewport, still reveals immediately with no scroll on a tall 1440x2200 viewport where the whole hero fits, item count (15), no horizontal overflow at 390/768/1440px, mobile shows the full list with no animation, keylines path count (15), hero centering (v303 fix), and the one-line "Born in New Zealand > Made in USA" eyebrow (v304 fix) -- none of that code was touched.

Previous version (v306): The v305 ingredient-list stagger reveal (each item fading in on the gold connector line's draw-in) was firing the instant any sliver of the hero touched the screen, so on a normal laptop-height window it had usually already finished before the visitor scrolled down far enough to actually see the list -- it read as "shows but doesn't animate" even though it had, a couple of seconds earlier, while the list was still off-screen. v306 delayed the trigger until the ingredient list itself had actually scrolled substantially into view.

Changes in v306:
- Bug fix: the reveal was gated on the whole `.hero-lower` block (headline, tag line, everything) becoming 15% visible, which -- because that block starts right at the top of the page -- was already true the instant the page loaded on most screens, well before the ingredient list itself (lower in that same block) had scrolled into view. Confirmed against a user-supplied screen recording: the stagger genuinely did play, correctly, but only across the first ~2 seconds after page load, while the visitor's attention (and, per the recording, their scroll position) was still on the headline/hero video above the list. Fixed by splitting what was one `IntersectionObserver` into two: one still watches the whole hero block at the original 15% threshold, but now only for resetting the reveal (so scrolling back up later replays it cleanly) -- and a second, new observer watches the ingredient list (`.hero-actives`) itself, at a 50% threshold, and is the only thing that starts the reveal. On a normal-height screen the list is below the fold at load, so the reveal now waits for the visitor to actually scroll it into view; on a screen tall enough to fit the whole hero at once, it still plays immediately with no scroll needed, same as v305 intended. Once started, the reveal stays visible as the visitor keeps scrolling on down the page -- it does not flicker back off just because the list's visibility ratio drifts back under 50% while they're reading further content below it; it only resets once they've scrolled essentially the entire hero out of view, matching the original re-entry/replay design.
- Verified in headless Playwright at a realistic 1440x900 viewport: the list's `active-in` classes are all still false immediately after page load (list starts ~35% into the viewport, under the new 50% bar), then reveal in the correct 90ms-per-item stagger once the list is scrolled to. A taller 1440x2200 viewport (whole hero fits at load) still reveals immediately with no scroll. Also verified the reveal survives continued scrolling past the list without resetting, resets cleanly once the whole hero is scrolled well out of view, and replays with a genuine staggered opacity gradient (not an instant jump) on scrolling back up to it. Re-checked: item count (15), no horizontal overflow at 390/768/1440px, mobile shows the full list with no animation, keylines path count (15), hero centering (v303 fix), and the one-line "Born in New Zealand > Made in USA" eyebrow (v304 fix) -- none of that code was touched.

Previous version (v305):
- Content: the hero ingredient list (`.hero-actives`) was missing 2 of the 15 named actives already counted in the "15 Full dose Natural actives" stat directly below it on the same page -- **Balanced Hydration Matrix electrolytes** and **AlphaWave&reg; L-Theanine** -- both already listed in the Stack section and AskHoni's own "What's in HONIORA?" answer, just never added to this specific list. Added both, so the hero list's own item count (15) now matches the stat it sits beside.
- New interaction: at the user's request ("use this scroll trigger to load all the ingredients onto the page"), each ingredient now fades/slides in individually as the visitor scrolls the hero into view, on the same 90ms-per-item stagger the gold connector lines already used to fan out to each one -- so the ingredient text and its own gold line now complete together, item by item, instead of the whole 15-item list simply appearing at once via the site-wide fade-in it used to share with the rest of the hero. Implemented by extending the existing gold-keylines script (`.hero-actives li.active-in` toggled by the very same `IntersectionObserver`/`STAGGER_MS` mechanism already driving the lines) rather than reusing the pasted video-scrub code verbatim, since that code scrubs video frames against continuous scroll position and has no built-in mapping onto revealing list text -- a discrete, staggered reveal keyed to scroll-into-view was the closer match to what the gold lines already do. Desktop-only, matching the gold lines themselves; the lite/mobile layout shows the full list immediately with no animation, and `prefers-reduced-motion: reduce` shows it immediately too.
- Bug fix (found while building the above): the script's own geometry-resync step, used to keep the gold lines' endpoints pinned to each ingredient's real position while nearby elements are still settling into place, was clearing each line's individual stagger delay every time it ran, by toggling the CSS `transition` shorthand off and back on -- setting a shorthand property and then clearing it wipes every longhand it expands to, transition-delay included, even one set independently beforehand. In practice this meant the gold lines' own stagger was silently collapsing into one simultaneous reveal on nearly every normal page load, well before this version. Fixed by toggling `transition-property` alone instead, which doesn't touch delay. A second, related issue surfaced once the delay was actually preserved: that same resync step runs on every animation frame for up to ~1.1s after any nearby element's own reveal, and forces an instant, no-transition jump to the final state each time it fires -- which, now that delays were finally respected, meant it was cutting the newly-working stagger short on its very first frame. Fixed by having that resync step stand down for the ~1.8s the staggered reveal itself needs, deferring to right after it finishes rather than fighting it. Both fixes are inside the pre-existing gold-keylines script, not new code paths, and both were necessary for the new per-item reveal (and, it turns out, the original gold-line stagger) to actually be visible rather than silently collapsing into an instant snap.
- Checked at 390px, 768px and 1440px: no horizontal overflow. Re-verified: hero centering (v303 fix), the one-line "Born in New Zealand > Made in USA" eyebrow (v304 fix), and the AskHoni header height/centering and reply-scroll-to-top behaviour (v304 fixes) are all still correct -- none of that code was touched. The gold-line stroke-dashoffset animation itself could only be confirmed correct at the CSS level in this headless test environment (computed `transition-delay` values are provably staggered per item, 0/90/180/...ms, matching the working ingredient-text stagger built on the identical mechanism) rather than visually frame-by-frame, since headless Chromium does not reliably render SVG stroke transitions smoothly; the underlying fix is the same one already confirmed visually staggered for the ingredient text, so it's expected to render correctly in a real browser.

Previous version (v304):
- Copy/layout: "Born in New Zealand > Made in USA" (the hero eyebrow line under the HONIORA wordmark) is now forced to a single line (`#bornMadeLine{white-space:nowrap}`) at every screen width, instead of wrapping to two lines on narrow phones as it did before. Paired with the same measure-real-width-and-shrink-only JS safety net (`fitOneLine`) used elsewhere in this hero, with an 8px floor, so forcing it to one line shrinks the text just enough to keep fitting rather than overflowing. Verified across 11 widths from 360px to 1440px: never wraps, never overflows its column at any of them.
- Bug fix: AskHoni's reply thread used to always jump to `scrollHeight` (the absolute bottom) after any message was added, including the bot's own reply. For a reply taller than the visible thread pane -- which the "What's in HONIORA?" answer regularly is, especially on a phone-height screen -- that scrolled straight past the start of the answer, landing the reader on its last few lines with the opening cut off above the fold. Bot replies now scroll only far enough that the reply's own top edge lands at the top of the visible thread (measured via `getBoundingClientRect`, not `offsetTop`, so it doesn't depend on which ancestor ends up as the positioned `offsetParent`), so every answer is read starting from its beginning. The user's own typed/clicked question keeps the old scroll-to-bottom behaviour, since it's short and that's what keeps it visible while the reply is still pending. Verified with a long reply ("What's in HONIORA?") on a realistic mobile viewport (390x844, thread pane 237px tall against a 309px-tall reply): the reply's top now lands exactly at the thread's top (0px offset), versus roughly 72px of the reply being scrolled off above the fold under the old behavior.
- Layout: the AskHoni panel's header (`.askhoni-head`) is roughly 38-40% shorter on a phone-width screen (measured 93.3px before, 58px after) via tighter padding (14px/12px down to 5px/4px), a smaller title (17px to 14px) and subtitle (12px to 10px, tighter line-height), and a proportionally smaller close button (34px to 26px). Desktop, where the subtitle now fits on one line instead of two at the smaller size, ends up roughly 51% shorter (93.3px to 46px) -- more than asked, but still legible and proportionate; flagging it in case that reads as too tight and needs easing back up.
- Bug fix: "Hi I'm Honi" (the AskHoni panel's title) wasn't actually centered in the header despite `justify-content:center` on its own flex row -- that row inherited the header's asymmetric padding (20px left, 54px right, the extra 34px on the right reserved so the subtitle's longer text clears the close button), so centering within it landed the title about 17px left of the header's true center. Fixed with a matching `margin-right:-34px` on the title only, which widens its box back to a symmetric 20px/20px frame so centering inside it lands on the header's real center; the subtitle is untouched and keeps its full clearance from the close button. Verified: title's own center point now exactly matches the header's center point at both mobile and desktop widths.
- Checked at 390px, 768px and 1440px: no horizontal overflow. Re-verified AskHoni chips still return correct answers (no KB or matcher code touched).

Previous version (v303):
- Bug fix: on a real, non-maximized Chrome window at roughly 1400-1450px wide (confirmed by direct DOM measurement against the user's own screenshot from their MacBook Air), the "HONIORA" hero wordmark (`h1.hero-logo`) rendered wider than the copy column above it (the "HUGE pills.../UPGRADE YOUR RITUAL/HoniOra's Solution.../2 fizzy tablets..." block), even though every element in that column has `text-align:center`. Root cause: the wordmark's font-size is set by a CSS `clamp(60px,10.6vw,152px)` sized off the full viewport width, not the actual copy column, and at this specific window-width range the column itself is squeezed to roughly 265-330px (a fixed 430px right-padding reserved on `.hero-lower`, plus the image column taking 62.7% of what's left). At that column width, 10.6vw of a ~1425px viewport is still ~151px, nowhere near the clamp's 60px floor, so the CSS never shrinks it. Unlike the two adjacent hero lines ("UPGRADE/YOUR/RITUAL" and the "HUGE pills..." line), which already had a runtime JS safety net (`fitOneLine`) measuring real rendered width against the real available column width and shrinking the font only enough to fit, the wordmark had no equivalent protection, so it overflowed its column and (having no `table-layout:fixed` trick to pin it in place, the way `.precede-table` does) grew rightward from the shared left edge instead of staying put and clipping/centering in place — breaking the shared center line every other hero line sits on. Fix: extended the exact same `fitOneLine` mechanism to the wordmark (new `id="heroWordmark"`, floor of 32px so it never reads as body text), running before the existing `matchHeroTagWidth()` step (which measures the wordmark's *current* rendered size to scale "MĀNUKA CREATINE PROTOCOL" to match it, so a shrink here is what that step then matches against). Verified by directly measuring `getBoundingClientRect()` center points for the hero-precede block, the wordmark, and the tag line across 13 viewport widths from 390px to 2560px, including 1425px (the user's actual reported window width) — all three now report the identical center at every width, where before the wordmark's center sat measurably to the right of the others in the 1400-1450px range specifically. Also re-verified AskHoni still functions correctly (no code near it was touched) and mobile/tablet layouts are unaffected.
- Checked at 390px, 768px and 1440px: no horizontal overflow.

Previous version (v274):
- Added White et al. 2016, Nutrients, PMID 26797633, to L-Theanine's citations (AskHoni KB entry and the stack-matrix row), alongside the existing Moulin 2024 and Evans 2021 references. Independently verified via the publisher's own page (PubMed itself returned a CAPTCHA wall to direct fetch): a randomised, double-blind, placebo-controlled crossover trial, 34 healthy adults, at HONIORA's exact 200 mg dose. It found lower subjective stress and lower cortisol after a multitasking stressor, but no cognitive-performance gain on the task itself, and its posterior alpha-wave increase showed up mainly in participants with higher trait anxiety, not correlated with the stress/cortisol drop. Both the KB entry and the stack-row copy state the mixed result plainly rather than only citing the positive finding.
- Checked at 390px, 768px and 1440px: no horizontal overflow.

Previous version (v271):

Hytolive's addition and D-Allulose's removal offset exactly, so unit weight (5,474.50 mg/tab), serving weight (10,949 mg), batch mass (766.430 kg) and die diameter (25mm) are all unchanged from the currently-published v7.7 figures. Named ingredient count stays at 28 (Hytolive takes D-Allulose's former slot rather than adding a 29th).

Previous version (v268):
- "UPGRADE / YOUR / RITUAL" (`.precede-lead`) switched from Arial (browser-synthesized fake italic) to `Playfair Display` bold italic, the same face already used for the HONIORA wordmark, so the line now genuinely matches the logo instead of just leaning the same direction. Font-size ceiling raised from `min(11.2vw,144px)` to `min(13vw,168px)` on desktop and from `clamp(44px,14vw,62px)` to `clamp(54px,16vw,78px)` on lite/mobile. Note for narrower desktop widths (roughly 1200-1500px, where a 13" MacBook Air sits): the existing JS safety net (`fitOneLine`) that shrinks this line just enough to keep it on one line without overflowing the two-column hero layout still applies, so the size increase is modest there (about 2-3px) — the column is the limiting factor at that width, not the new CSS ceiling. The size gain is much more visible on mobile and on wider desktop screens (1900px+).
- Wrapped the entire hero-precede copy ("HUGE pills...", "UPGRADE YOUR RITUAL", "HoniOra's Solution:", "2 fizzy tablets...") in a single-cell `<table role="presentation">` (an invisible, borderless 1x1 layout table) instead of each line being centered independently by its own `text-align:center`. All four lines now resolve their position as one atomic layout unit tied to the table's single column width, so they stay centered on each other through resizes, breakpoint changes, and the webfont swap, rather than each span settling into place on its own. Used `table-layout:fixed` so the table can't grow past its declared width to accommodate the bigger text (a table's default auto-layout will do that, which broke both the two-column layout and the JS shrink safety net during testing — caught and fixed before shipping).
- Verified at 390px, 768px, 1280px (MacBook Air), 1366px, 1440px and 1920px: no horizontal overflow at any width, and the "UPGRADE YOUR RITUAL" block's horizontal center now matches the HONIORA logo's center exactly at every one of those widths (measured programmatically, not just eyeballed) — previously this was only incidentally true because both elements happened to be full-width blocks; now it's structural.

Recent changes in v261:
- Checked the hero headline block's centering at 12 widths from 360px up to 1920px, across every layout mode the site has (stacked phone/tablet, the 1001-1400px hybrid range, and the full desktop two-column layout). At every width, every line of "HUGE pills and/or chalky, messy powders....Every morning?", "UPGRADE / YOUR / RITUAL", "HoniOra's Solution:", "2 fizzy tablets & 1 glass of water & 60 sec.", "HONIORA" and "MĀNUKA CREATINE PROTOCOL" measured as sitting dead-centre in its column (equal left/right space either side), confirmed both by direct pixel measurement and by screenshot at 390px, 800px, 1200px and 1920px.
- That centering was previously working correctly, but relied entirely on one shared CSS rule several DOM levels up (`.hero-precede{text-align:center}`) being inherited down through nested spans, some of which (`precede-lead`, holding the three-line "UPGRADE/YOUR/RITUAL" stack, and `#precedeHuge`) are `display:inline-block`, where inheritance is easy to accidentally break with a future edit. Added an explicit `text-align:center` directly on each of those inner spans (`.precede-lead`, `.precede-rest`, `.precede-body`, `.precede-solution`, `#precedeHuge`) and on `.hero-tag`, so the centering no longer depends on inheritance surviving future changes elsewhere in the stylesheet. No visual change from this version — it's the same centered layout as before, just locked in place.

- Found and fixed a real bug in the mobile/tablet dropdown menu: from about 700px wide up to 1400px, the two gold "Reserve" hex buttons in the header grow up to 160px tall, well past the header bar's own 111px height, but the menu's top clearance was a fixed 128px. Bumped the menu's top clearance for that width range so it always clears the hexes at their tallest. Checked at 390px (phone, unaffected, still fine), 600px, 800px and 1000px (all now clear).

Recent changes in v254 (the promoted nav redesign, formerly on the experiment branch):
- Mānuka Honey copy: cut the overlapping sentence from the first lede paragraph (the crystal form negating sugar load / concentrating the MGO core), since the "Reduced Glycemic Burden" paragraph right below it already covers that same ground in more detail. Also applied to the numbered line as v253.
- Removed the heavy gold vertical line you circled. Turned out to be two things stacked on each other: the vertical bracket I'd drawn at the list's edge in the last pass (removed), plus a 2px solid gold left border that was already part of that ingredient list's styling before any of this keylines work (also removed, since nothing else on the page uses that particular list style, so it's safe to drop).
- Fixed the fan lines not landing exactly on their ingredients. The math was already exact at rest, but the ingredient list fades/slides into place on scroll (a 0.9s reveal animation), and the lines only re-synced once at the very END of that animation -- so for most of a second after scrolling into view, every line pointed at each ingredient's pre-reveal position, about 26px off, which is almost certainly what you saw in your screenshot. The lines now track the list's real position on every animation frame during that reveal, so they stay pixel-perfect throughout it, not just once it settles. Verified zero drift at 50ms, 300ms, 600ms, 1s and 2.5s after scrolling the section into view.
- Fixed a real bug: the hero's gold keylines never appeared on a plain page load. The script only played the draw-in animation on scrolling back up into an already-visited hero, so a visitor landing fresh on the homepage (the overwhelmingly common case) saw no lines at all until they scrolled all the way past the hero and back up -- it looked like the feature was simply missing. Now the very first in-view check plays them too.
- Put the hero's gold keylines back to the single-point starburst fan from the tube (matching your reference mock), and added the vertical bracket detail at the OTHER end this time: a plain vertical bar at the ingredient list's own left edge, spanning its top-most to bottom-most row, capped top and bottom with a short horizontal tick, which is where all 13 fan lines land (my previous pass had put that bracket at the tube end by mistake). Same script, same scroll-triggered draw-in, same "off entirely below 1000px" lite behaviour. Checked at 1920px and confirmed the lines render immediately on a cold load with no scrolling.
- Added a hover popup to every nav word, not just the four CTA items. Protocol, Rita Rocks, Function, Stack, Cr.01 Creatine, Mānuka Honi, Livaux, About Honico, Honi Blog and Ritual all now show a short one-line explainer on hover, using the same dropdown card style and the same copy source as their own section on the page. Checked at 1600px and at 1410px (just above the mobile breakpoint) to confirm none of the cards run off either edge of the screen.
- Lightened the honeycomb pattern twice more (each pass blended halfway toward white: #DCC89C to #EEE4CE to #F6F2E6) and shrunk the hexagons 50 percent (radius 34 to 17, tile now 51x29.44px instead of 102x58.89px), so it reads as a fine, faint texture rather than a bold pattern. Same tileable SVG technique throughout, just paler and smaller hexagons.
- Rebuilt the whole desktop nav bar to match the hexhead_template mockup you sent: every link and CTA is now its own plain text label sitting directly on a pale gold honeycomb background (no more embossed gold hex button images), in the exact order and wording from your mockup — Reserve Honiora, Protocol, Rita Rocks, Function, Test Tube, Stack, CR.01 Creatine, the HONIORA logo centred, then Mānuka Honi, Livaux, About Honico, Honi Blog, Ask Honi, Ritual, Join Honi. Livaux is a new nav item that didn't exist before (linked to the Stack/ingredients section since there's no dedicated Livaux anchor on the page — flag it if you want it pointed somewhere else). The four items that carry a hover dropdown (Reserve Honiora, Test Tube, Ask Honi, Join Honi) kept their popups, and I re-measured and re-centred the little pointer arrow on the two edge ones (Reserve Honiora, Join Honi) for the new text-based trigger width so it still points at the right button rather than a neighbour. The honeycomb pattern itself is a lighter/pale gold now (matching your hexhead_base reference) rather than the deeper gold from the last nav pass. Checked desktop widths from 1410px up to 1920px (including the narrow edge just above the mobile breakpoint, where the edge dropdowns could most easily overflow), the scrolled "stuck" state, and confirmed the tablet/mobile hamburger nav below 1400px is untouched — it still uses the old embossed hex Reserve buttons, just on the new lighter background.

Recent changes in the numbered v25x line (carried into this branch's starting point):

No other part of the section (photo, three pillars, timeline facts) touched.

Recent changes in this version:
- Found and fixed a real bug you spotted: the Join List popup was pointing at Ask Honi instead. Both end buttons (Reserve Protocol on the left, Join List on the right) have popup cards nearly 2.5x wider than the hex button itself, so they're deliberately shifted sideways to avoid running off the edge of the screen, sitting mostly under the NEXT button over rather than centred on their own trigger. The little arrow on the card was still centred on the card's own middle by default, so it ended up pointing at that neighbour (Ask Honi) instead of the button that actually opened it (Join List). Re-centred the arrow on the real trigger's own middle for both end buttons, confirmed with hover tests: Join List's popup now visibly points at Join List, and I found and fixed the same latent issue on Reserve Protocol (was pointing toward Reserve Test Tube) while I was in there. The two middle buttons (Reserve Test Tube, Ask Honi) were already correct and untouched.

Recent changes in this version:
- Redid the four gold hex nav badges a shade darker and richer (was reading a bit bright/yellow-gold, now a deeper bronze-gold), same wax-stamp bevel and engraving otherwise.
- Checked nav bar spacing and found a real bug: the right-hand cluster (Ask Honi / Join List) was overflowing about 34px past where it should end, so "Join List" sat closer to the edge than "Reserve Protocol" on the mirror side of the bar even though the underlying grid was set up to keep both sides equal. Root cause: the two flanking nav columns were plain equal-width grid tracks (1fr/1fr), which split the leftover space evenly without first checking whether each side's own content (its two hex buttons and nav links) actually fit in that share; since the right side's links ("Mānuka Honey", "About HoniCo") run a bit longer than the left side's ("Protocol", "Stack"), its content didn't fit and just spilled out past its own column instead of the column growing to hold it. Fixed by giving each column a floor equal to its own content's width, so it can still share extra space evenly but never gets squeezed smaller than what's actually in it. Confirmed the left and right edge padding now match exactly (both sides) at 1440px, 1600px and 1920px wide. One honest side effect: because the two sides' nav-link text still isn't the same width, the HONIORA logo now sits about 6px left of dead-centre rather than exactly centred, versus overflowing content before. That's a few pixels on a 1600px-wide bar, not something a visitor would notice, and far better than content spilling past the edge.

All four now have a lite-specific version sized and compressed for how big they actually render on a phone (hero-packshot-cutout: 1.9MB &rarr; ~120KB, manuka-coast: 1.1MB &rarr; ~130KB, globe: 772KB &rarr; ~60KB), picked automatically via the same `<source media>` technique used everywhere else on the site, plus the missing lazy-load fixed on the globe's lightbox copy.

Recent changes in this version:
- The hero video is back on the lite site, and fast: a new, purpose-made encode, `hero-glass-lite.mp4` / `hero-glass-lite.webm`, downscaled to 400px wide (the size it actually displays at on a phone) and re-compressed at a lower bitrate. 267KB / 388KB versus the desktop file's 2.6MB / 1.2MB, a roughly 85-90% cut. Autoplay is back on for every device: the lite site picks this small file automatically (the same `<source media>` technique already used for the Mānuka banner and Rita Rocks lite images), so it loads and starts playing quickly without competing with everything else on the page.

Recent changes in this version:
- Found why the lite site was slow, and why the tube, the beekeeper banner and Rita Rocks seemed not to load: the hero video (tablets fizzing in the glass) had `autoplay` and `preload="auto"` on every device, so it started pulling down several megabytes the instant the page opened even on a phone, eating the bandwidth everything further down the page was waiting on. It now only preloads and plays on desktop and the 1001-1400px tablet layout; on the lite site it downloads nothing at all and just shows its static poster frame permanently, freeing up the connection for the images that actually need it. Also found and removed a second, older piece of code that was calling `.play()` on this same video unconditionally on every device (an earlier iOS Safari fix) -- it would have silently started downloading the video on the lite site regardless of the new gating above, so it had to go too.

Recent changes in this version:
- Found the real cause of the jump/double-up right where the beekeeper (Mānuka) banner ends and the ingredient scroll section begins: the banner's image was lazy-loaded, and its "finished loading" event was wired to force a ScrollTrigger recalculation. Since that image sits right before the pinned scroll section, it was finishing its lazy load exactly while the visitor was scrolling into that section, and recalculating a pinned ScrollTrigger mid-scrub snaps it to a corrected position, which reads as a jump or a momentary doubled-up frame. The banner image now loads eagerly (no more `loading="lazy"` on it) and no longer triggers a recalculation on load, so it's fully loaded and settled long before anyone scrolls near it. This is the exact same failure mode already avoided for the ingredient tile images themselves (see the code comment next to it), just missed on the banner image at the time.

Recent changes in this version:
- Hero keylines, round two. Found and fixed a real alignment bug: the ingredient list carries this page's own scroll-reveal treatment (fades and slides up into place, at a slightly different moment for the headline, the tag line, the list, and the CTAs), so the first geometry measurement was taken before that settled and every line was landing about 26px above its ingredient. Lines now re-measure and correct themselves the moment that settling finishes, confirmed by direct pixel comparison against each ingredient's own tick mark: 0px off on desktop and the lite site, under half a pixel on mobile (rounding, invisible).
- Also found and fixed a second, more serious bug from the first attempt at this correction: a safety timer meant to catch slow-loading fonts was unconditionally re-hiding the lines a couple of seconds after page load, even if the visitor had already scrolled up and was actively looking at them. Rebuilding the geometry (on resize, on that settle, as a backstop) now always reapplies whatever state was actually showing, instead of assuming hidden.
- Belt and suspenders against "not loading" happening a third way: the whole script now runs inside a try/catch that logs to the browser console instead of failing silently, and people with "reduce motion" set on their device now get the lines permanently drawn and visible with no animation, rather than no lines at all, so a system accessibility setting can no longer look identical to a broken feature. If it's still not showing after this, a hard refresh clears a stale cached copy of the page, and the browser console (right-click, Inspect, Console tab) will show a "[hero-keylines]" message if something on that specific device is still going wrong.

Recent changes in this version:

Recent changes in this version:
- Fixed: the gold hero keylines from v229 weren't loading. The likely cause was the GSAP/ScrollTrigger CDN script (loaded from cdnjs.cloudflare.com) failing or being blocked somewhere in the chain, since that's exactly what happened in my own test environment when I re-checked it. Rebuilt the whole feature on plain vanilla JS and CSS instead (IntersectionObserver plus a CSS transition), with zero dependency on that or any other external library, so it can no longer be taken down by a blocked or slow third-party script. Re-verified end to end with GSAP deliberately unavailable, confirming it still works: 13 lines build, draw in on scroll-up, and reset on scroll-down.
- Tightened the alignment: every line now ends at the exact same left edge and vertical offset already used by that ingredient's own small gold tick mark (the existing dash to the left of each ingredient's name), so each line lands precisely on its ingredient rather than an approximation of its position. Re-verified at 390px, 1200px and 1600px with a screenshot at each: every line visibly terminates right at its ingredient's tick, tube to ingredient, correctly at every size including the lite and stacked mobile layouts.

Recent changes in this version:
- New: gold "keylines" in the hero. Thin gold lines now connect the tube's cap end to each of the 13 active ingredients in the list beside it, drawing in one by one as the visitor scrolls back UP through the hero after having scrolled further down the page. They reset to hidden if you scroll back down away from the hero, so the reveal replays each time you scroll up into it again. Built with GSAP ScrollTrigger (already loaded on this page for the "the unit" horizontal-scroll section), so it only turns on if that library loads and the visitor doesn't have "reduce motion" set in their OS, in which case the page just looks like it did before, no broken half-state. Positions are measured from the real, live layout rather than assumed, so this works unmodified on the lite site and the stacked mobile layout too, not just desktop. Checked at 390px, 1200px and 1600px: 13 lines build correctly at each, draw in on scroll-up, and reset on scroll-down.

Recent changes in this version:

Recent changes in this version:
- "FROM THE LAND OF THE LONG WHITE CLOUD" (the caption on the Mānuka vista banner photo) is bigger: desktop went from a 16-34px range to 20-44px, lite/mobile from 14-20px to 18-26px. While making it bigger, found and fixed a real pre-existing bug: on desktop, this caption was rendering at 0px tall (confirmed via computed styles, not just a screenshot guess) because it inherits `line-height:0` from its parent banner container (`.manukapan{line-height:0}`, there to remove the thin gap under the banner photo) and never had its own line-height to override it — so the text was actually collapsing invisibly at the very top of the banner, mostly hidden behind the nav bar, on every version of the site before this one, not something this font-size change introduced. Mobile was unaffected, it already had its own `line-height:1.25` for its wrapped multi-line layout. Added `line-height:1.2` to the desktop rule, confirmed the caption now has real height (53px at 1600px wide, was 0px) and is fully visible across the top of the photo, checked at 390px and 1600px, no overflow off either edge of the image.

Recent changes in this version:
- Fixed the tube's vertical centering on desktop. The previous version centered it with a small fixed margin near the video, but the desktop column actually stretches to match the full height of the ingredient list, stats, and CTA button on the right (`.hero-lower` uses `align-items:stretch`), so that fixed margin left a huge dead gap below the tube once the page rendered at full height. The tube now uses auto top/bottom margins instead, which soak up whatever space is actually left in the stretched column and center it there for real, wherever that column happens to end. Confirmed at 1600px: space above and below the tube now match, and the ingredient list, stats, and Explore button are all still clear of it. Lite (≤1000px) is unaffected, it never had this stretched-column situation to begin with.

v220 changes:
- The gold tube shot is now rotated 90° clockwise (lying on its side, cap to the right) and sized to a "life size" proportion relative to the glass: a note on that judgment call, since a screen has no fixed physical size, true life-size in centimeters isn't something a web page can guarantee across devices. What's implemented instead is a realistic real-world ratio — a ~13cm effervescent tube next to a ~15cm pint glass — so the tube reads as roughly true-to-scale beside the glass rather than a shrunken icon. Worth verifying against the actual tube and glass dimensions if exact scale matters for this placement. It's centered with equal margin above and below in the space between the glass and the copy that follows (on lite that's literally the "UPGRADE YOUR RITUAL" headline below it; on desktop the copy runs alongside this column rather than under it, so the same equal-margin treatment centers it in the column's own whitespace instead). Checked 390px, 820px, 1000px, 1401px, and 1600px: no overlap with the ingredient list or headline copy at any of them.
- The gold tube product shot sits directly under the fizzing glass video in the hero, on both desktop and lite. New file `hero-pack-tube.png`: background fully removed and recomposited on pure white (no grey halo or vignette anywhere near the edges, checked at pixel level), cropped tight to the tube itself.
- "MĀNUKA CREATINE PROTOCOL" now matches "HONIORA"'s exact rendered width on desktop. A runtime script measures both lines' true text width (off-screen DOM probes, accounting for font, weight, style, and letter-spacing, not just the elements' box width) and scales the tag line's font-size to match. Checked 1401px through 2560px: rendered widths land within a third of a pixel of each other at every width. Lite is untouched — confirmed the script leaves the tag's font-size alone below 1401px, where its own separate clamp rule still governs.

v216 changes:
- On the lite site, the two mobile hex nav buttons (Reserve Protocol, Reserve Test Tube) lost the "i" info badge added a few versions back. In its place, the whole button now doubles in size on hover/focus, growing outward away from the logo (not over it) rather than from dead centre, so it never climbs on top of the wordmark. The info card itself is unaffected for anyone hovering with a real pointer (trackpad on a tablet-width screen already got it on hover before this change); it's specifically the tap-a-badge path for pure touch that's gone, replaced by the size cue. Checked at 390px: badge is gone, both buttons scale cleanly on hover with no overlap.
- Fixed a real layout bug on the lite site: the Founders Offer section (the pricing intro just above the plan cards) had a large empty gap between the offer text and the five-tube pack photo below it, on every phone and tablet width up to 820px. Cause: the text column's CSS gave it `flex-basis: 480px`, meant to set its *width* when it sits side by side with the photo on wider screens, but on narrower screens where that row stacks into a column, flex-basis controls *height* instead, so the same 480px was forcing the text block to a minimum 480px tall regardless of how short its actual text was, no matter the content. Reset that for the stacked layout so the block just takes its natural height, closing the gap. Checked up to 820px, the photo now sits right below the text at every width in that range, and the side-by-side desktop layout above 820px is completely unaffected (that flex-basis:480px is doing its original, correct job there).

v214 changes:

v213 changes:
- The hero headline's big lead line is back to "UPGRADE YOUR RITUAL" (reverted from the "UPGRADE YOUR DAY" wording tried in v212) and now always breaks onto exactly three lines, UPGRADE, YOUR, and RITUAL, one word per line, on both desktop and the lite site, and set bigger again on top of that (desktop's cap went 58px to 72px, lite's went 50px to 62px). A script keeps it safe at every width: it measures the widest of the three lines against its real column width at runtime and only scales the font down, never up, if a single word would otherwise run past the edge, since the desktop and lite layouts give this line very differently sized columns and a single hand-tuned size can't be trusted to fit both. Checked 320px through 2560px: three lines every time, nothing overflows or overlaps the glass image, and re-verified on resize and after the webfont loads.

v209 changes:
- In the 09 The Stack table, Cr-01 Creatine's row got two real citations in place of the "Ingredient science citation to be added, verify before launch" placeholder. Checked both before using them: Gutierrez-Hellin et al. 2025 in Nutrients, "Creatine Supplementation Beyond Athletics: Benefits of Different Types of Creatine for Women, Vegans, and Clinical Populations," a narrative review confirming this is a real, indexed paper directly about creatine's benefits beyond sports. The second, a 2026 paper in The Journal of Nutritional Physiology, "Creatine supplementation and brain health," I confirmed is real and indexed (also listed on ScienceDirect and a university repository), but the full text is paywalled everywhere I could check, so I couldn't read the actual findings, only the title, which is why the citation on-site is labeled by year and journal rather than claiming specific results from it. Also dropped the opening sentence about creatine being the largest single ingredient by weight, and added "trusted and" ahead of "heavily researched," both per your edit.

v208 changes:
- Resolved the hypotonic vs isotonic contradiction from the last version. You confirmed the drink is hypotonic at the actual serving size (2 tablets in 300 ml, which is what the site's own dosing instructions already say). I ran a rough order-of-magnitude check against the real formula, summing the dissolved particles from every major active (sodium and potassium from the bicarbonates, magnesium, creatine, taurine, D-allulose, the citrate/malate left over from the effervescent reaction) and came out around 250 mOsm/L, below blood plasma's roughly 285 to 295 mOsm/kg, which is directionally consistent with hypotonic. That's an estimate from ingredient masses, not a lab osmometer reading, so treat it as a sanity check, not a certified number. The one remaining site mention that said "Isotonic electrolyte matrix" now says "Hypotonic electrolyte matrix," matching the other spot that already said "slightly hypotonic drink." I didn't add the more specific "accelerates absorption of creatine, amino acids, and botanical extracts" claim from what you sent, hypotonic solutions generally do empty the stomach faster than isotonic ones, that's well established, but I don't have a source tying that specifically to this formula's ingredients, so I left it as the general "sits easy" language already there rather than a specific absorption-speed claim.

v207 changes:
- Repositioned HONIORA as a daily health booster rather than a gym electrolyte-replacement or pre-workout product. Went through the whole site for sports/gym-coded language and softened or reframed about a dozen spots: the "Cellular Energy" and "Clean Hydration" hero pillar blurbs (dropped "clinical saturation payloads," "neuromuscular performance," "precision osmology," "isotonic fluid delivery system," "GI distress"), the Strength/Gut/Heart/Brain matrix section lede (dropped "power output" emphasis and "The HONIORA protocol performs"), the Dissolve section lede (dropped "high performance"), the "Rapid hydration and nutrient delivery" heading and body (now "Fast-dissolving, easy to absorb," dropped "rushing nutrients into your bloodstream"), the VasoDrive-AP heading (now "Better blood flow, better circulation," was "Upgraded blood flow and muscle delivery"), the creatine section heading and body (now "Cellular energy, every day"), a section header ("The Depth of a Natural Active Antipodean Matrix," was "The Power of..."), and every mention of creatine as an "ergogenic nutrient" or for "sports nutrition/sports science" (five places: the Strength quadrant paragraph, the stack-matrix row, the Ask Honi creatine answer twice, the ishot caption, and the Jenerise origin-story lede), reframed instead around cellular energy and healthy aging, matching your own "Essential for All, Not Just Athletes" video framing. Landkind's "training stress and recovery" / "training load" language (four places: ishot caption, stack-matrix row, ingredient detail block, Ask Honi answer) is now "everyday stress." Left alone on purpose: the "Strength" category name itself (core to the site's Strength/Gut/Heart/Brain architecture, renaming it is a much bigger job than tone), "effervescent engine" as a phrase (your own term for the fizzing mechanism, not gym language), and a few spots that describe actual study findings ("exercise recovery," "exercise-induced muscle damage" in Braincurrant's citations) since rewording those would misstate what the cited research covered. The disputed "hypotonic" vs "isotonic" wording from the last version is still there, still unresolved, still needs your call on which one's correct.

v206 changes:

For Taurine, the whole source line was that disclaimer and nothing else, so the source line is gone entirely for that one now. For Zinc and Landkind, the disclaimer was trimmed off the end of their manufacturer line, leaving just the manufacturer information. Nothing else about these three entries changed.

v205 changes:
- Expanded the science behind Bifidobacterium adolescentis in the Ask Honi answer for it, covering how its short-chain fatty acids (like butyrate) connect to the blood-brain barrier and to creatine uptake, not just the gut. You supplied four science-style claims about this; each was independently checked before anything went on-site, same as every other ingredient here. What held up: gut inflammation has been shown (in a rat/mouse study) to cut expression of a nutrient transporter at the blood-brain barrier, and a real 2024 human study found a probiotic reducing a gut compound that otherwise blocks the intestinal creatine transporter, raising circulating creatine, plus a solid 2023 review on how short-chain fatty acids protect the blood-brain barrier generally. What didn't: no evidence turned up for gut bacteria improving taurine absorption specifically, so that claim was dropped, and the creatine-transporter finding is from a different Bifidobacterium species (B. animalis, not our B. adolescentis) tested in older sarcopenia patients, so it's flagged on-site as a different strain and population rather than presented as evidence for this product. Also left out: the suggestion that this ties in with cGP-Pro and VasoDrive-AP specifically, since that particular combination hasn't actually been studied together. Three new citations added to the Ask Honi panel for this entry, six total now, all checked against the actual paper content.

v204 changes:

v203 changes:
- On the lite site, the three fine-print notes under the pricing plans ("Free USA shipping, always.", "First Drop, 2026.", "Founding list only.") no longer have huge empty gaps between them on an iPhone. The cause: those three paragraphs share a CSS rule meant for the desktop layout, where they sit side by side and each one has a 220px minimum width. On phones the layout switches to stacking them vertically, but that same 220px number was still being applied, and on a stacked (vertical) layout the browser reads it as a minimum height instead of a width, forcing every paragraph into a 220px-tall box no matter how short its actual text was. Reset that so each note's height on mobile matches its own text again. Checked 320px through 1600px: the desktop side-by-side layout is unchanged, and every phone width now shows the three notes stacked tightly with even spacing.

- Fixed the hex nav sizing so it actually shows up on vertical (portrait) phones, not just when rotated to landscape. Previously the v175 size increase only applied once the screen got wide enough to enter the "tablet" nav layout, which portrait phones almost never reach, so portrait phones looked unchanged. Now portrait phones get their own two-step growth curve: hexes stay at their existing safe size on the narrowest phones (360-390px, where there's no room to grow without touching the burger icon), then grow meaningfully from 391px up to 560px wide, capping around 106-118px on the widest phones, versus 65px before. Checked from 360px to 560px, no overlap with the burger anywhere.
- Fixed landscape phones getting oversized. Rotating a phone to landscape used to push it past the 560px "phones" width and into the tablet-sized nav (up to 160px hexes, 110px bar), which was far too big for a short landscape screen. Landscape phones (width taller than they are, but under 500px tall) now get their own compact sizing, 70px bar and hexes around 40-43px, similar to portrait. Tablets and desktops in landscape (which have much more height, 600px+) are unaffected and keep the full-size nav. Checked several real phone landscape sizes (iPhone SE through iPhone Pro Max) plus iPad/desktop landscape to confirm the cutoff doesn't catch anything it shouldn't.
- Reordered the right-hand column of the two-column mobile menu: The Stack, Gut · Heart · Brain, Taste, Ask Honi, and About HoniCo now sit together at the bottom of that column, in that order, after the other links (2 Tabs, Ritual, Join the Founding List, Rita Rocks, Lemonwater?, HoniBlog), which keep their previous relative order above them. Nothing else in that section changed, and the Jenerise/Steve Jennings sourcing story is still told elsewhere on the page (the Cr-01 ingredient row and the Stack matrix), just not repeated a second time here.

v169 changes:
- On the lite (mobile) site, the nav bar itself is taller (92px, up from 78px on the main lite range; 76px, up from 64px, on phones), the HONIORA wordmark is bigger (58px, up from 48px on the main lite range; 38px, up from 36px, on phones), and the two hex buttons are bigger too (92px, up from 78px on the main lite range; up to 62px, up from 56px, by 500px-wide phones). On the narrowest phones (360-430px) the hexes hold roughly their existing size rather than growing, since the bigger logo now takes up more of the space between it and the burger icon and there just isn't room for both to grow at that width without them touching. Checked from 360px up through 1300px: no overlap with the logo or burger anywhere, no horizontal overflow, logo stays perfectly centred, and desktop (1401px+) is unchanged.

v168 changes:
- The mobile-nav "Reserve Protocol" and "Reserve Test Tube" hexes are bigger again (78px, up from 64px on the main lite range, and up to 56px on phones, from 50px), and a real cascade bug is fixed along the way: the phone-only size and spacing rules were losing to the wider lite-range rules because of a specificity mismatch, so shrinking the hexes down for narrow phones was silently not working. Fixed the selector specificity and re-tuned the phone-width sizing and spacing so the bigger hexes still clear the burger icon and the logo with no overlap and no horizontal overflow, checked from 360px up through 1300px. Also centred the "15,220 mg / 5,000 mg / 15" stats row under the hero on mobile: it used to wrap raggedly, left-aligned, once the layout stacks to one column, now it stacks as one centred group like everything else in that section, on desktop it's unchanged.

v167 changes:
- The mobile nav now puts one hex on each side of the logo (Reserve Protocol on the left, Reserve Test Tube on the right) instead of both together on one side. The logo itself is now always truly centred on the bar (same 1fr/auto/1fr grid technique the desktop nav uses), so it no longer drifts off-centre depending on hex sizing. Also fixed a bug this introduced along the way, where the mobile hexes were briefly showing up as a stray second row underneath the desktop nav above 1400px; that's cleaned up, desktop is back to a single clean row. In "01 / The Ritual" hero copy, added line breaks after "messy powders," "every morning," and "HoniOra's Solution:" on mobile only, desktop keeps the original flowing paragraph. Checked from 360px up through the full lite range and on desktop, no overlap with the logo or burger and no horizontal overflow at any normal phone width.

v166 changes:
- The mobile-nav "Reserve Protocol" and "Reserve Test Tube" hexes are bigger again (64px, 50px on phones, up from 56px/44px) and now sit close beside the logo at every lite/mobile width, not just on narrow phones: they used to be pinned to the screen's left edge, so the gap to the logo grew wider the wider the screen got (up to ~220px on tablet-width "lite" views). They now move as one group with the logo, centred together, so the gap to the logo stays a small, consistent ~20-28px everywhere from 375px up to the 1400px desktop handoff. Checked for overlap against the logo and burger icon at 375, 390, 430, 560, 900 and 1300px, and confirmed the hexes still tap through to the store section.

v165 changes:
- Fixed a real bug in the mobile slide-out menu: the 16-link list is taller than most phone screens, but the menu panel had no scrolling enabled, so it just vertically centred the list and clipped whatever didn't fit, links cut off with no way to reach them. The panel now scrolls properly (tested that every link from "2 Tabs" down to "HoniBlog" is reachable), with room at the top to clear the still-visible header and room at the bottom so the last link isn't flush against the screen edge. Checked on several phone sizes (375, 390, 430px, including a short-screen iPhone SE-sized viewport), no horizontal overflow introduced.

v164 changes:
- The mobile-nav "Reserve Protocol" and "Reserve Test Tube" hexes (added in v163) are bigger and better spaced apart: 40 percent taller (56px, 44px on phones, up from 40px/32px) with real breathing room between them (14px/10px gap, up from a slight overlap). Checked at 375, 390, 430, 900 and 1300px: still clears the centred logo with room to spare and no horizontal overflow.

v163 changes:

Both tap through to the store section. Checked at 375px, 390px, 430px and the wider "lite" widths up to 1400px: no overlap with the logo or burger icon, and no horizontal overflow. The full-size desktop hex buttons above 1400px are unchanged.

v162 changes:

Its background is now genuinely transparent (proper alpha channel, letters re-matted so there's no white fringe at the edges), so it blends cleanly into the header no matter what's showing behind it as you scroll, instead of showing a hard white rectangle. Checked against several backdrop colours and against the live header in both its transparent and "stuck" (scrolled) states.

v161 changes:

v159 changes:

v158 changes:
- Fixed a real hero-section layout bug reported on a MacBook Air: at common laptop widths (roughly 1000px to 1600px), the "Upgrade your ritual" copy and the actives list were squeezed into a tiny, wrapped, near-centered column instead of the intended wide left-aligned layout. Root cause was the hero's text block sharing a CSS class with the page's standard side-padding, which was eating width it shouldn't have inside the hero's own layout. Fixing that width alone then exposed a second, previously-hidden problem: with the text column properly widened, the glass image (which is intentionally shifted right to sit close to the copy) started visually overlapping the headline and ingredient list. Both are now fixed together: the text column has its full intended width, and the glass image and text no longer overlap at any desktop width from 1001px up to 2560px+ (spot-checked at 1001, 1280, 1366, 1401, 1440, 1600, 1920, 2560). Also re-checked mobile (375, 390, 430px): type and images are centred as intended and there is no horizontal overflow/side-scroll anywhere on the page.

Jenerise Cr-01 Creatine Monohydrate is built to stricter and higher standards than its competitors: independently tested and verified by SGS at 99.96% purity."

The heading now uses a smaller, dedicated size for this specific three-line heading, so it renders as exactly the three intended lines at every desktop width (checked 1401px to 2560px). v145 changes:

That offset is now cleared on mobile; the desktop layout is unaffected.

v118 changes — reworked the mobile hero, on request for a "lite" mobile layout (built into the same responsive index.html rather than a second file, so there's nothing extra to host):
- Mobile hero order changed: glass video, then the logo/tagline/bullet-list/stats text block, then the Pure Origin pillar leading the other three pillars (Cellular Energy, Vascular Support, Clean Hydration) — previously the pillars sat between the video and the text block.
- Glass video enlarged 50% on mobile (190px → 285px max-height).
- Fixed a real autoplay/loop bug: the script meant to force-start the hero video on browsers that block autoplay was querying a leftover class name (`.glassbox`) that no longer exists anywhere in the page, so that fallback silently did nothing. It now points at the real video element, so the loop reliably starts on phones that need the manual nudge (notably iOS Safari).

v117 changes — a full mobile audit ("is the mobile site screen-width only, and is everything centered"):
- Fixed real horizontal overflow on phones: the four-label "Strength → Gut → Heart → Brain" loop diagram and the ingredient-table group headings ("Effervescent & organoleptic system" etc.) were both set to stay on one line no matter how narrow the screen, so on phones they silently pushed the whole page a few dozen pixels wider than the screen. Both now wrap properly on narrow screens; the page no longer scrolls sideways at any phone width tested (375–430px).
- Fixed a real bug, not just cosmetic: the entire 09 TheStack ingredient table (all 27 actives) was wrapped in a single scroll-reveal animation that only turns visible once 12% of its own height has scrolled into view. That threshold was written for normal-sized blocks — this one block is far taller than any phone screen, so that 12% was mathematically impossible to reach and the whole section stayed invisible on mobile. The reveal now triggers off a small pixel amount instead of a fixed percentage, so it still displays at the same point for every normal-sized block on the page, but now works correctly for this one too.
- Confirmed all page sections and images are properly centered (the `.wrap` container was already using `margin:0 auto`) — the "not centered" feeling was actually the sideways scroll bug above; once that's fixed, everything sits centered as intended.

v113 changes:
- Fixed the pinned horizontal-scroll "unit" section (the claims list and the ingredient-photo band): the ingredient images were each triggering a layout refresh the instant they finished loading, and because they load lazily while that section is pinned, those refreshes were firing mid-scroll and causing the scroll position to snap or jump. The unnecessary refreshes are removed; the section now scrubs smoothly start to finish.

- 07 Function table reduced in width and centered.
- "No Maltodextrin" removed from the claims list in the pinned scroll section.
